```
17	const maxHTTPErrorBody = 64 * 1024
18	
19	// httpError checks an HTTP response status and returns an error for non-2xx responses.
20	func httpError(res *http.Response) error {
21		if res.StatusCode >= http.StatusOK && res.StatusCode < http.StatusMultipleChoices {
22			return nil
23		}
24		var reason string
25		if res.Body != nil {
26			limited := io.LimitReader(res.Body, maxHTTPErrorBody+1)
27			data, err := io.ReadAll(limited)
28			if err == nil && len(data) > 0 {
29				if len(data) > maxHTTPErrorBody {
30					data = append(data[:maxHTTPErrorBody], []byte("...")...)
31				}
32				reason = string(data)
33			}
34		}
35		return fmt.Errorf("http %d: %s %s", res.StatusCode, res.Request.URL.Redacted(), reason)
36	}
37	
38	// StatsPhaseHeader is the HTTP header used to annotate requests with the
39	// current git-sync stats phase for round-trip tracking.
40	const StatsPhaseHeader = "X-Git-Sync-Stats-Phase"
41	
42	// AuthMethod authorizes outbound HTTP requests for a remote. It is satisfied
43	// by *transporthttp.BasicAuth and *transporthttp.TokenAuth, whose Authorizer
44	// methods replaced the AuthMethod interface that go-git removed in v6 alpha.2.
45	type AuthMethod interface {
46		Authorizer(req *http.Request) error
47	}
48	
49	// Conn represents a connection to a remote Git HTTP endpoint.
50	type Conn struct {
51		Label    string
52		Endpoint *url.URL
53		HTTP     *http.Client
54		Auth     AuthMethod
55	
56		// FollowInfoRefsRedirect, when true, rewrites Endpoint.Scheme and
57		// Endpoint.Host to the final URL returned by RequestInfoRefs after
58		// HTTP redirects. Subsequent PostRPC* calls then target the
59		// redirected host directly, matching vanilla git's smart-HTTP
60		// behaviour for discovery-aware servers that 307 /info/refs to a
61		// hosting replica. Endpoint.Path is never modified — it still
62		// contains the repo path. Off by default to preserve behaviour for
63		// callers that rely on Endpoint being stable.
64		FollowInfoRefsRedirect bool
65	}
66	
67	// NewConn creates a new connection to the given endpoint.
68	func NewConn(ep *url.URL, label string, auth AuthMethod, rt http.RoundTripper) *Conn {
69		httpClient := &http.Client{Transport: rt}
70		return NewConnWithHTTPClient(ep, label, auth, httpClient)
71	}
72	
73	// NewConnWithHTTPClient creates a new connection using the provided HTTP client.
74	// Passing nil falls back to a default client and is intended only for direct
75	// callers outside git-sync's normal instrumented session setup.
76	func NewConnWithHTTPClient(ep *url.URL, label string, auth AuthMethod, httpClient *http.Client) *Conn {
77		if httpClient == nil {
78			httpClient = &http.Client{Transport: http.DefaultTransport}
79		}
80		normalizeEndpointPath(ep)
81		return &Conn{
82			Label:    label,
83			Endpoint: ep,
84			HTTP:     httpClient,
85			Auth:     auth,
86		}
87	}
88	
89	func normalizeEndpointPath(ep *url.URL) {
90		if ep == nil {
91			return
92		}
93		ep.Path = strings.TrimRight(ep.Path, "/")
94		ep.RawPath = strings.TrimRight(ep.RawPath, "/")
95	}
96	
97	// NewHTTPTransport creates an http.Transport with optional TLS skip.
98	func NewHTTPTransport(skipTLS bool) http.RoundTripper {
99		if !skipTLS {
100			return http.DefaultTransport
101		}
102		if cloned, ok := http.DefaultTransport.(*http.Transport); ok {
103			tc := cloned.Clone()
104			if tc.TLSClientConfig == nil {
105				tc.TLSClientConfig = &tls.Config{MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS12}
106			}
107			tc.TLSClientConfig.InsecureSkipVerify = true
108			return tc
109		}
110		return http.DefaultTransport
111	}
112	
113	// RequestInfoRefs fetches /info/refs for the given service.
114	func RequestInfoRefs(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, service string, gitProtocol string) ([]byte, error) {
115		reqURL := fmt.Sprintf("%s/info/refs?service=%s", conn.Endpoint.String(), service)
116		req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, reqURL, nil)
117		if err != nil {
118			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create info-refs request: %w", err)
119		}
120		req.Header.Set("Accept", "*/*")
121		req.Header.Set("User-Agent", capability.DefaultAgent())
122		req.Header.Set(StatsPhaseHeader, service+" info-refs")
123		if gitProtocol != "" {
124			req.Header.Set("Git-Protocol", gitProtocol)
125		}
126		ApplyAuth(req, conn.Auth)
127	
128		res, err := conn.HTTP.Do(req)
129		if err != nil {
130			return nil, fmt.Errorf("request info-refs: %w", err)
131		}
132		defer res.Body.Close()
133		if err := httpError(res); err != nil {
134			return nil, err
135		}
136		wantContentType := fmt.Sprintf("application/x-%s-advertisement", service)
137		gotContentType := res.Header.Get("Content-Type")
138		gotMediaType := gotContentType
139		if gotContentType != "" {
140			if mediaType, _, err := mime.ParseMediaType(gotContentType); err == nil {
141				gotMediaType = mediaType
142			}
143		}
144		if gotMediaType != wantContentType {
145			return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected info/refs content-type %q, want %q", gotContentType, wantContentType)
146		}
147		if conn.FollowInfoRefsRedirect && res.Request != nil && res.Request.URL != nil {
148			final := res.Request.URL
149			if final.Host != conn.Endpoint.Host || final.Scheme != conn.Endpoint.Scheme {
150				conn.Endpoint.Scheme = final.Scheme
151				conn.Endpoint.Host = final.Host
152			}
153		}
154		// Bound the read to prevent unbounded memory allocation (issue #9).
155		const maxInfoRefsSize = 64 * 1024 * 1024 // 64 MiB
156		lr := io.LimitReader(res.Body, maxInfoRefsSize+1)
157		data, err := io.ReadAll(lr)
158		if err != nil {
159			return nil, fmt.Errorf("read info-refs response: %w", err)
160		}
161		if int64(len(data)) > maxInfoRefsSize {
162			return nil, fmt.Errorf("info/refs response exceeds %d byte limit", maxInfoRefsSize)
163		}
164		return data, nil
165	}
166	
167	// PostRPC sends a buffered POST to the given service and returns the full response body.
168	// Responses are bounded to prevent unbounded memory allocation (issue #9).
169	func PostRPC(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, service string, body []byte, v2 bool, phase string) ([]byte, error) {
170		reader, err := PostRPCStream(ctx, conn, service, body, v2, phase)
171		if err != nil {
172			return nil, err
173		}
174		defer reader.Close()
175		const maxRPCResponse = 128 * 1024 * 1024 // 128 MiB
176		lr := io.LimitReader(reader, maxRPCResponse+1)
177		data, err := io.ReadAll(lr)
178		if err != nil {
179			return nil, fmt.Errorf("read RPC response: %w", err)
180		}
181		if int64(len(data)) > maxRPCResponse {
182			return nil, fmt.Errorf("RPC response for %s exceeds %d byte limit", service, maxRPCResponse)
183		}
184		return data, nil
185	}
186	
187	// PostRPCStream sends a POST to the given service and returns the response body
188	// as a streaming reader. Caller must close the returned ReadCloser.
189	func PostRPCStream(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, service string, body []byte, v2 bool, phase string) (io.ReadCloser, error) {
190		return PostRPCStreamBody(ctx, conn, service, bytes.NewReader(body), v2, phase)
191	}
192	
193	// PostRPCStreamBody sends a POST to the given service using a streaming request body.
194	// Caller must close the returned ReadCloser.
195	func PostRPCStreamBody(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, service string, body io.Reader, v2 bool, phase string) (io.ReadCloser, error) {
196		reqURL := fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s", conn.Endpoint.String(), service)
197		req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, reqURL, body)
198		if err != nil {
199			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create RPC request: %w", err)
200		}
201		req.Header.Set("Content-Type", fmt.Sprintf("application/x-%s-request", service))
202		req.Header.Set("Accept", fmt.Sprintf("application/x-%s-result", service))
203		req.Header.Set("User-Agent", capability.DefaultAgent())
204		req.Header.Set(StatsPhaseHeader, phase)
205		if v2 {
206			req.Header.Set("Git-Protocol", "version=2")
207		}
208		ApplyAuth(req, conn.Auth)
209	
210		res, err := conn.HTTP.Do(req)
211		if err != nil {
212			return nil, fmt.Errorf("post RPC: %w", err)
213		}
214		if err := httpError(res); err != nil {
215			_ = res.Body.Close()
216			return nil, err
217		}
218		return res.Body, nil
219	}
220	
221	// ApplyAuth applies the given auth method to an HTTP request. Errors from
222	// the Authorizer (e.g. transient signing failures) are surfaced as request
223	// failures by leaving the Authorization header unset; the upstream server
224	// will reject with 401 and the caller logs the surrounding context.
225	func ApplyAuth(req *http.Request, auth AuthMethod) {
226		if auth == nil {
227			return
228		}
229		_ = auth.Authorizer(req) //nolint:errcheck // BasicAuth and TokenAuth never error; future authorizers should surface 401s instead
230	}
```

```
100	// sendReceivePack encodes and POSTs a receive-pack request, then decodes the report.
101	func sendReceivePack(
102		ctx context.Context,
103		conn *Conn,
104		req *packp.UpdateRequests,
105		packData io.Reader,
106		verbose bool,
107	) error {
108		var header bytes.Buffer
109		if err := req.Encode(&header); err != nil {
110			return fmt.Errorf("encode update-request: %w", err)
111		}
112		body := io.Reader(bytes.NewReader(header.Bytes()))
113		if packData != nil {
114			body = io.MultiReader(body, packData)
115		}
116		reader, err := PostRPCStreamBody(ctx, conn, transport.ReceivePackService, body, false, "receive-pack push")
117		if err != nil {
118			return fmt.Errorf("target receive-pack: %w", err)
119		}
120		defer reader.Close()
121	
122		// Unwrap sideband if negotiated; stream server-side progress to stderr
123		// when verbose so long-running pushes show "Resolving deltas ..." etc.
124		var respReader io.Reader = reader
125		switch {
126		case req.Capabilities.Supports(capability.Sideband64k):
127			dem := sideband.NewDemuxer(sideband.Sideband64k, reader)
128			dem.Progress = progressSink(verbose, "target: ")
129			respReader = dem
130		case req.Capabilities.Supports(capability.Sideband):
131			dem := sideband.NewDemuxer(sideband.Sideband, reader)
132			dem.Progress = progressSink(verbose, "target: ")
133			respReader = dem
134		}
135	
136		if req.Capabilities.Supports(capability.ReportStatus) {
137			report := packp.NewReportStatus()
138			if err := report.Decode(respReader); err != nil {
139				return fmt.Errorf("decode report-status: %w", err)
```

```
443	
444	type roundTripperFunc func(*http.Request) (*http.Response, error)
445	
446	func (f roundTripperFunc) RoundTrip(req *http.Request) (*http.Response, error) {
447		return f(req)
448	}
449	
450	type roundTripReader struct {
451		remaining int
452	}
453	
454	func (r *roundTripReader) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
455		if r.remaining <= 0 {
456			return 0, io.EOF
457		}
458		n := len(p)
459		if n > r.remaining {
460			n = r.remaining
461		}
462		for i := range n {
463			p[i] = 'x'
464		}
465		r.remaining -= n
466		return n, nil
467	}
468	
469	func (r *roundTripReader) Close() error { return nil }
470
```

> AGENT

The current `httpError` already reads up to 64KB of the response body — so "Internal Server Error" is the actual body content. What's missing are response headers that often carry trace/correlation IDs (especially useful here since the URL is on Cloudflare's artifacts service — the `Cf-Ray` header is the key for them to look up the failure server-side).

Let me look at existing tests and a couple more spots before patching.

Surfaced in\n// httpError so a 500 with an opaque body (e.g. \"Internal Server Error\") still\n// gives the user something actionable to share when reporting the failure.\nvar diagnosticHeaders = []string{\n\t\"Cf-Ray\",\n\t\"X-Request-Id\",\n\t\"Request-Id\",\n\t\"X-Trace-Id\",\n\t\"X-Amz-Request-Id\",\n\t\"X-Github-Request-Id\",\n\t\"Server\",\n\t\"Content-Type\",\n}\n\n// httpError checks an HTTP response status and returns an error for non-2xx responses.\nfunc httpError(res *http.Response) error {\n\tif res.StatusCode >= http.StatusOK && res.StatusCode < http.StatusMultipleChoices {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tvar reason string\n\tif res.Body != nil {\n\t\tlimited := io.LimitReader(res.Body, maxHTTPErrorBody+1)\n\t\tdata, err := io.ReadAll(limited)\n\t\tif err == nil && len(data) > 0 {\n\t\t\tif len(data) > maxHTTPErrorBody {\n\t\t\t\tdata = append(data[:maxHTTPErrorBody], []byte(\"...\")...)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treason = strings.TrimSpace(string(data))\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tvar diag []string\n\tfor _, h := range diagnosticHeaders {\n\t\tif v := res.Header.Get(h); v != \"\" {\n\t\t\tdiag = append(diag, h+\"=\"+v)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif len(diag) > 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"http %d: %s [%s] %s\", res.StatusCode, res.Request.URL.Redacted(), strings.Join(diag, \", \"), reason)\n\t}\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"http %d: %s %s\", res.StatusCode, res.Request.URL.Redacted(), reason)\n}"
```json
}
```

> AGENT

Builds and passes. The patched `httpError` now appends any present trace/correlation headers (`Cf-Ray`, `X-Request-Id`, `Server`, `Content-Type`, etc.) to the error message and trims trailing whitespace from the body. With the change, your error would look more like:

```
http 500: https://...host.../git-receive-pack [Cf-Ray=8abc..., Server=cloudflare, Content-Type=text/plain] Internal Server Error
```

The `Cf-Ray` value is the key one for Cloudflare's artifacts service — share it with them and they can look up exactly which backend request failed and why.

A couple of caveats worth noting:
- If the server isn't sending any of those headers, the message format is unchanged from before.
- If the body really was just the literal text "Internal Server Error" with no extra info (which is what you saw), the headers are likely the only place useful detail is hiding. If even those come back empty, the next step would be to capture the raw response with `--verbose` or via `mitmproxy` / `GIT_CURL_VERBOSE`-style tracing to inspect what the server is actually returning.

Want me to also add a verbose/debug-only dump of all response headers when the request fails, gated by an existing flag?

```
164	func ensureEntireRepo(t *testing.T, baseURL, repoName string, skipTLSVerify bool) error {
165		t.Helper()
166	
167		bin, err := entireCLIBinary()
168		if err != nil {
169			return err
170		}
171	
172		listCmd := exec.CommandContext(t.Context(), bin, "repo", "list")
173		listCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), entireCLIEnv(baseURL, skipTLSVerify)...)
174		output, err := listCmd.CombinedOutput()
175		if err != nil {
176			return fmt.Errorf("list repos with entiredb: %w\n%s", err, strings.TrimSpace(string(output)))
177		}
178		for _, line := range strings.Split(string(output), "\n") {
179			if strings.TrimSpace(line) == repoName {
180				return nil
181			}
182		}
183	
184		createCmd := exec.CommandContext(t.Context(), bin, "repo", "create", repoName)
185		createCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), entireCLIEnv(baseURL, skipTLSVerify)...)
186		output, err = createCmd.CombinedOutput()
187		if err != nil {
188			return fmt.Errorf("create repo with entiredb: %w\n%s", err, strings.TrimSpace(string(output)))
189		}
190		return nil
191	}
192	
193	func entireCLIBinary() (string, error) {
194		if path := os.Getenv("GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIREDB_BIN"); path != "" {
195			return path, nil
196		}
197		if path, err := exec.LookPath("entiredb"); err == nil {
198			return path, nil
199		}
200		return "", errors.New("could not find entiredb; set GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIREDB_BIN or put entiredb on PATH")
```

```
README.md:1:![git-sync theme](docs/images/gh-repo-cover.png "git-sync cover image")
README.md:3:# git-sync
README.md:5:`git-sync` mirrors refs from a source remote to a target remote without creating a local checkout. It uses an in-memory `go-git` object store and talks smart HTTP directly:
README.md:17:`git-sync` fills that gap. It streams source packs directly into target `receive-pack` when it can, plans every action before pushing, and emits typed JSON for automation.
README.md:25:- `git-sync sync`: mirror source refs into the target
README.md:26:- `git-sync replicate`: overwrite target refs to match source via relay, and fail rather than materialize locally
README.md:28:`sync` automatically bootstraps an empty target, so the same command covers initial seeding and ongoing sync. To preview what would happen without pushing, run `git-sync plan` — it takes the same flags as `sync`, and `--mode replicate` previews a `replicate` run.
README.md:34:`git-sync` is also a Go library. Use `entire.io/entire/git-sync` for the stable embedding surface (`Probe`, `Plan`, `Sync`, `Replicate`, typed results, auth and HTTP injection). `entire.io/entire/git-sync/unstable` exposes advanced controls (`Bootstrap`, `Fetch`, batching knobs, heap measurement) and is not stable.
README.md:42:brew install --cask git-sync
README.md:50:go install entire.io/entire/git-sync/cmd/git-sync@latest
README.md:53:This drops a `git-sync` binary into `$(go env GOPATH)/bin`. Make sure that directory is on your `PATH`.
README.md:58:git clone https://github.com/entireio/git-sync.git
README.md:59:cd git-sync
README.md:60:go build -o git-sync ./cmd/git-sync
README.md:66:git-sync sync \
README.md:77:`sync` picks the bootstrap relay path automatically when the target is empty. For non-empty targets, safe fast-forward updates also use a relay path that streams the source pack directly into target `receive-pack` without local materialization. Anything not relay-eligible (force, prune, deletes, tag retargets) falls back to a materialized path bounded by `--materialized-max-objects`.
README.md:93:- [docs/usage.md](docs/usage.md) — CLI commands, examples, sync behavior, JSON output, auth, protocol notes
README.md:100:### Does it sync complete Git history or only perform a shallow/partial sync?
README.md:102:`git-sync` syncs the complete Git object history required for the selected refs. It does not create a shallow clone. Some planning paths may use filtered fetches, but the target receives the full objects needed for valid refs.
README.md:106:Objects as well. Refs are what `git-sync` plans and updates, but it also transfers the commits, trees, blobs, and tags needed for those refs to exist on the target.
README.md:110:No. `git-sync` is one-way: source remote to target remote. To go the other way you'd run a second invocation with the endpoints swapped.
README.md:114:Yes. It supports creating refs, updating refs, force updates with `--force`, and deleting managed refs with `--prune`. `replicate` can overwrite target refs, but it is relay-only and more restrictive than `sync`.
README.md:118:`git-sync` has two transfer paths:
README.md:121:- **Materialized fallback** — when relay isn't available, `git-sync` fetches the needed objects into an in-memory `go-git` store, plans, then encodes and pushes a packfile. Memory scales with the diff being pushed and is guarded by an explicit object-count limit. Bootstrap can batch large initial syncs to keep this bounded.
README.md:133:No. `git-sync` supports smart HTTP/HTTPS only.
README.md:137:No. `git-sync` is a one-shot CLI/library operation. To sync on a schedule or in response to events, run it from cron, CI, a worker, or another service.
```

> AGENT

You already have a turnkey smoke test for exactly this — it defaults its source to `https://github.com/entireio/cli.git`. Two paths depending on what you want to validate:

## Path 1 — full end-to-end against your local Entire (recommended)

This actually pushes the cli.git repo through `git-sync` into a local Entire instance, then probes both sides and asserts the ref hashes match.

```sh
# in /Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv (parent dir)
mise run dev   # starts entire backend on :8080, frontend on :5173

# in another terminal, in git-sync/
ENTIRE_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 mise run test:entire-local-smoke
```

What it does (`internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test.go:33`):
1. Reads your local Entire credentials via the keychain (`entiredb` must be on PATH, or set `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIREDB_BIN`)
2. Creates a `git-sync-smoke` repo on your local Entire if it doesn't exist
3. Runs `Run(...)` with source `https://github.com/entireio/cli.git`, branch `main`, target `http://localhost:8080/git/<user>/git-sync-smoke`
4. Probes both sides and fails if the ref hashes don't match

Override knobs (env vars): `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE_REPO`, `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE_BRANCH`, `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE_PROTOCOL=v2`, `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE_BATCH_MAX_PACK_BYTES=...`.

## Path 2 — quick CLI run against a local target

If you just want to drive the CLI binary you built and watch the logs:

```sh
go build -o git-sync ./cmd/git-sync

# get a token for your local entire user (test/password1234 by default)
TOKEN=$(entiredb auth token)   # or whatever your local helper is

./git-sync sync --tags \
  --target-username git \
  --target-token "$TOKEN" \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git \
  http://localhost:8080/git/test/test-repo-cli
```

If the local target repo doesn't exist yet, create it first with `entiredb repo create test-repo-cli` (or via the frontend at http://localhost:5173).

## Path 3 — reproduce the original 500 with the new error output

Just re-run your original command — the diagnostic-headers patch in `internal/gitproto/smarthttp.go` will surface `Cf-Ray=…`, `Server=…`, `Content-Type=…`, etc., next to the body text. That gives the Cloudflare-side operators something to look up in their logs.

For day-to-day validation that the relay/bootstrap path works on `cli.git`, **Path 1** is the most thorough — it asserts ref-hash equality between source and target, so you'll catch silent corruption, not just non-failing pushes.

> AGENT

Found it — the `git-sync-smoke` repo already exists on your local Entire (from a previous run), but the test's existence check doesn't notice. Two problems combined:

1. `entiredb repo list` doesn't print plain `name\n` lines — its output contains protobuf-style control bytes (`\016`, `\022\032`, `\r`) around each name, so `entire_local_smoke_test.go:178` never matches `git-sync-smoke` even though it's there.
2. The fallback `entiredb repo create git-sync-smoke` then fails with `AlreadyExists`, and cobra prints the usage block above the actual error — that's the message you saw.

## Quickest unblock — just rerun

```sh
ENTIRE_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 \
GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE_REPO=git-sync-smoke-$(date +%s) \
mise run test:entire-local-smoke
```

That uses a fresh repo name on every run, sidestepping both issues.

## Slightly tidier — reuse existing repo

```sh
ENTIRE_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 \
REDACTED \
mise run test:entire-local-smoke
```

…but only after deleting the stale one (or once the test is patched — see below). To delete:

```sh
entiredb repo delete git-sync-smoke   # check `entiredb repo --help` for exact syntax
```

## The real fix

Make `ensureEntireRepo` tolerate an already-existing repo. The check-then-create pattern is racey and brittle anyway; treating `AlreadyExists` as success is more robust than parsing `repo list` output. Want me to patch `internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test.go:164` so it inspects the create-command output for the "already exists" gRPC code and returns nil in that case?

```
41	type executor struct {
42		ctx    context.Context
43		params Params
44	}
45	
46	// Execute runs the materialized fallback: ensures tag objects are local,
47	// computes the object closure, and pushes to the target.
48	func Execute(ctx context.Context, p Params) error {
49		if len(p.PushPlans) == 0 {
50			return nil
51		}
52		return (&executor{ctx: ctx, params: p}).run()
53	}
54	
55	func (e *executor) run() error {
56		if err := e.ensureTagObjects(); err != nil {
57			return fmt.Errorf("prepare local objects for push: %w", err)
58		}
59		hashes, err := e.collectObjectClosure()
60		if err != nil {
61			return fmt.Errorf("compute objects to push: %w", err)
62		}
63		if err := e.enforceObjectLimit(hashes); err != nil {
64			return err
65		}
66		return e.push(hashes)
67	}
68	
69	func (e *executor) ensureTagObjects() error {
70		tagDesired := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef)
71		for _, plan := range e.params.PushPlans {
72			if plan.Kind != planner.RefKindTag {
73				continue
74			}
75			if d, ok := e.params.DesiredRefs[plan.TargetRef]; ok {
76				tagDesired[plan.TargetRef] = gitproto.DesiredRef{
77					SourceRef: d.SourceRef, TargetRef: d.TargetRef,
78					SourceHash: d.SourceHash, IsTag: true,
79				}
80			}
81		}
82		if len(tagDesired) == 0 {
83			return nil
84		}
85		err := e.params.SourceService.FetchToStore(e.ctx, e.params.Store, e.params.SourceConn, tagDesired, nil)
86		if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, git.NoErrAlreadyUpToDate) {
87			return fmt.Errorf("fetch tag objects to store: %w", err)
88		}
89		return nil
90	}
91	
92	func (e *executor) collectObjectClosure() ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
93		objects := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(e.params.PushPlans))
94		for _, plan := range e.params.PushPlans {
95			if plan.Action == planner.ActionCreate || plan.Action == planner.ActionUpdate {
96				objects = append(objects, plan.SourceHash)
97			}
98		}
99		hashes, err := planner.ObjectsToPush(e.params.Store, objects, e.params.TargetRefs)
100		if err != nil {
101			return nil, fmt.Errorf("compute objects to push: %w", err)
102		}
103		return hashes, nil
104	}
105	
106	func (e *executor) enforceObjectLimit(hashes []plumbing.Hash) error {
107		maxObjects := effectiveMaxObjects(e.params.MaxObjects)
108		if len(hashes) <= maxObjects {
109			return nil
110		}
111		return fmt.Errorf(
112			"materialized push requires %d objects (limit %d); use bootstrap for large initial syncs",
113			len(hashes), maxObjects,
114		)
115	}
116	
117	func (e *executor) push(hashes []plumbing.Hash) error {
118		cmds := convert.PlansToPushCommands(e.params.PushPlans)
119		if e.params.TargetPusher == nil {
120			return errors.New("materialized strategy requires TargetPusher")
121		}
122		if err := e.params.TargetPusher.PushObjects(e.ctx, cmds, e.params.Store, hashes); err != nil {
123			return fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
124		}
125		return nil
126	}
127	
128	func effectiveMaxObjects(limit int) int {
129		if limit > 0 {
130			return limit
131		}
132		return DefaultMaxMaterializedObjects
133	}
134
```

```
408	
409	// ObjectsToPush computes the set of objects that need to be sent to the target.
410	func ObjectsToPush(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, wants []plumbing.Hash, targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {
411		haveSet := make(map[plumbing.Hash]struct{})
412		for _, h := range targetRefs {
413			if !h.IsZero() {
414				haveSet[h] = struct{}{}
415			}
416		}
417	
418		filteredWants := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(wants))
419		for _, h := range wants {
420			if _, ok := haveSet[h]; !ok {
421				filteredWants = append(filteredWants, h)
422			}
423		}
424		if len(filteredWants) == 0 {
425			return nil, nil
426		}
427	
428		seen := make(map[plumbing.Hash]bool, len(filteredWants)*4)
429		objects := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(filteredWants)*16)
430		for _, h := range filteredWants {
431			if err := collectObjects(store, h, haveSet, seen, &objects); err != nil {
432				return nil, err
433			}
434		}
435		return objects, nil
436	}
437	
438	func collectObjects(
439		store storer.EncodedObjectStorer,
440		hash plumbing.Hash,
441		haves map[plumbing.Hash]struct{},
442		seen map[plumbing.Hash]bool,
443		out *[]plumbing.Hash,
444	) error {
445		if hash.IsZero() {
446			return nil
447		}
448		if _, ok := haves[hash]; ok {
449			return nil
450		}
451		if seen[hash] {
452			return nil
453		}
454		seen[hash] = true
455	
456		obj, err := store.EncodedObject(plumbing.AnyObject, hash)
457		if err != nil {
458			return fmt.Errorf("load object %s: %w", hash, err)
459		}
460	
461		switch obj.Type() {
462		case plumbing.CommitObject:
463			commit, err := object.GetCommit(store, hash)
464			if err != nil {
465				return fmt.Errorf("load commit %s: %w", hash, err)
466			}
467			if err := collectObjects(store, commit.TreeHash, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
468				return err
469			}
470			for _, ph := range commit.ParentHashes {
471				if err := collectObjects(store, ph, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
472					return err
473				}
474			}
475		case plumbing.TreeObject:
476			tree, err := object.GetTree(store, hash)
477			if err != nil {
478				return fmt.Errorf("load tree %s: %w", hash, err)
479			}
480			for _, entry := range tree.Entries {
481				if err := collectObjects(store, entry.Hash, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
482					return err
483				}
484			}
485		case plumbing.TagObject:
486			tag, err := object.GetTag(store, hash)
487			if err != nil {
488				return fmt.Errorf("load tag %s: %w", hash, err)
489			}
490			if err := collectObjects(store, tag.Target, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
491				return err
492			}
493		case plumbing.BlobObject:
494			// Blobs are leaf objects — nothing to recurse into.
```

> AGENT

This is a legitimate failure, not noise. The flow that hit you:

1. The target already has refs (so it's not a clean bootstrap target → bootstrap relay path skipped at `internal/syncer/syncer.go:476`).
2. `FetchToStore` (`syncer.go:497`) fetches with the target's ref hashes as **haves**. The server walks reachability and excludes any object reachable from those haves, so the returned pack is a minimal delta.
3. Incremental relay couldn't take the plan, so it fell back to materialized.
4. `materialized.collectObjectClosure` walks the source object graph using *only the literal target ref hashes* as haves (`internal/planner/planner.go:411`). It doesn't know about reachability — only exact hash matches.
5. Object `0fccf698…` is reachable from a target ref (and therefore wasn't sent in the pack), but it isn't itself one of the target ref hashes. So the walker tries to load it from the store and fails.

This is a genuine seam between the wire's reachability-based have-set and the walker's literal-only one. It only bites when the target has refs whose object closure overlaps with the source closure but the relay path can't be used.

## Easy ways to validate sync is working without hitting this

**1. Force the bootstrap path against the same artifacts target (cleanest signal)**

If you can delete `test-repo-cli` on the cloudflare target and recreate it empty, your next `git-sync sync …` will take the bootstrap relay path — that's the path your patch from earlier was about, and it bypasses the materialized walker entirely.

**2. Local Entire smoke test (already discussed)**

```sh
ENTIRE_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 \
GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE_REPO=git-sync-smoke-$(date +%s) \
mise run test:entire-local-smoke
```

This validates source→target equivalence end-to-end with `https://github.com/entireio/cli.git` and a guaranteed-fresh target, so it always exercises the bootstrap path.

**3. Re-run against an artifacts repo that's empty**

If the artifacts service supports it, point `$ARTIFACTS_REMOTE` at a brand-new repo that's never been pushed to. Same logic as (1).

## If you actually want to fix the underlying issue

The materialized walker's have-set should be reachability-closed, not just the literal target ref hashes. One of:

- Before walking, expand `targetRefs` into a full closure by asking source for those commits' objects (counterproductive — partly negates the bandwidth win of haves).
- Or: when `collectObjects` hits a missing object, treat the parent as a boundary instead of failing — i.e., assume the gap means the server already-have'd that subgraph. Risky if a real corruption exists.
- Or: switch the materialized strategy to skip the explicit walk and let `go-git`'s pusher compute the closure with proper haves negotiation.

For your immediate goal of "validate sync is working", I'd go with option 1 above (empty artifacts repo) — it tests your patched 500 path and avoids this orthogonal bug. Want me to dig into a real fix for the materialized/haves mismatch?

```
55	// buildUpdateRequest builds the receive-pack update request.
56	func buildUpdateRequest(
57		adv *packp.AdvRefs,
58		commands []PushCommand,
59		verbose bool,
60	) (*packp.UpdateRequests, bool, bool, error) {
61		req := packp.NewUpdateRequests()
62		if sb := PreferredSideband(adv.Capabilities); sb != "" {
63			if err := req.Capabilities.Set(sb); err != nil {
64				return nil, false, false, fmt.Errorf("set capability: %w", err)
65			}
66		}
67		if adv.Capabilities.Supports(capability.ReportStatus) {
68			if err := req.Capabilities.Set(capability.ReportStatus); err != nil {
69				return nil, false, false, fmt.Errorf("set capability: %w", err)
70			}
71		}
72	
73		hasDelete := false
74		hasUpdates := false
75		for _, cmd := range commands {
76			c := &packp.Command{Name: cmd.Name, Old: cmd.Old}
77			if cmd.Delete {
78				c.New = plumbing.ZeroHash
79				hasDelete = true
80			} else {
81				c.New = cmd.New
82				hasUpdates = true
83			}
84			req.Commands = append(req.Commands, c)
85		}
86	
87		if hasDelete {
88			if !adv.Capabilities.Supports(capability.DeleteRefs) {
89				return nil, false, false, errors.New("target does not support delete-refs")
90			}
91			if err := req.Capabilities.Set(capability.DeleteRefs); err != nil {
92				return nil, false, false, fmt.Errorf("set capability: %w", err)
93			}
94		}
95	
96		_ = verbose // progress handling is server-side in HTTP mode
97		return req, hasDelete, hasUpdates, nil
98	}

100	// sendReceivePack encodes and POSTs a receive-pack request, then decodes the report.
101	func sendReceivePack(
102		ctx context.Context,
103		conn *Conn,
104		req *packp.UpdateRequests,
105		packData io.Reader,
106		verbose bool,
107	) error {
108		var header bytes.Buffer
109		if err := req.Encode(&header); err != nil {
110			return fmt.Errorf("encode update-request: %w", err)
111		}
112		body := io.Reader(bytes.NewReader(header.Bytes()))
113		if packData != nil {
114			body = io.MultiReader(body, packData)
115		}
116		reader, err := PostRPCStreamBody(ctx, conn, transport.ReceivePackService, body, false, "receive-pack push")
117		if err != nil {
118			return fmt.Errorf("target receive-pack: %w", err)
119		}
120		defer reader.Close()
121	
122		// Unwrap sideband if negotiated; stream server-side progress to stderr
123		// when verbose so long-running pushes show "Resolving deltas ..." etc.
124		var respReader io.Reader = reader
125		switch {
126		case req.Capabilities.Supports(capability.Sideband64k):
127			dem := sideband.NewDemuxer(sideband.Sideband64k, reader)
128			dem.Progress = progressSink(verbose, "target: ")
129			respReader = dem
130		case req.Capabilities.Supports(capability.Sideband):
131			dem := sideband.NewDemuxer(sideband.Sideband, reader)
132			dem.Progress = progressSink(verbose, "target: ")
133			respReader = dem
134		}
135	
136		if req.Capabilities.Supports(capability.ReportStatus) {
```

```
149	func PushObjects(
150		ctx context.Context,
151		conn *Conn,
152		adv *packp.AdvRefs,
153		commands []PushCommand,
154		store storer.Storer,
155		hashes []plumbing.Hash,
156		verbose bool,
157	) error {
158		req, _, hasUpdates, err := buildUpdateRequest(adv, commands, verbose)
159		if err != nil {
160			return err
161		}
162		if !hasUpdates {
163			return sendReceivePack(ctx, conn, req, nil, verbose)
164		}
165	
166		useRefDeltas := !adv.Capabilities.Supports(capability.OFSDelta)
167		pr, pw := io.Pipe()
168		done := make(chan error, 1)
169	
170		go func() {
171			enc := packfile.NewEncoder(pw, store, useRefDeltas)
172			if _, err := enc.Encode(hashes, 10); err != nil {
173				done <- pw.CloseWithError(fmt.Errorf("encode packfile: %w", err))
174				return
175			}
176			done <- pw.Close()
177		}()
178	
179		err = sendReceivePack(ctx, conn, req, pr, verbose)
180		_ = pr.Close()
181		encodeErr := <-done
182		if err != nil {
183			return err
184		}
185		return encodeErr
186	}
187	
188	// PushPack pushes a pack stream (relay) to the target.
189	func PushPack(
190		ctx context.Context,
191		conn *Conn,
192		adv *packp.AdvRefs,
193		commands []PushCommand,
194		pack io.ReadCloser,
195		verbose bool,
196	) error {
197		for _, cmd := range commands {
198			if cmd.Delete {
199				_ = pack.Close()
200				return errors.New("pack push only supports create and update actions")
201			}
202		}
203	
204		req, _, _, err := buildUpdateRequest(adv, commands, verbose)
205		if err != nil {
206			_ = pack.Close()
207			return err
208		}
209	
210		err = sendReceivePack(ctx, conn, req, pack, verbose)
211		closeErr := pack.Close()
212		if err != nil {
213			return err
214		}
215		if closeErr != nil {
216			return fmt.Errorf("close pack: %w", closeErr)
217		}
218		return nil
219	}
220

224		conn *Conn,

226		commands []PushCommand,

228	) error {
```

> AGENT

So go-git has `revlist.Objects(store, wants, haves)` which computes the closure correctly, but it requires the haves' objects to be in the local store. The fake test server uses it because it has the full repo locally. In our materialized strategy we don't, and that's the seam.

Let me confirm the failure mode with a focused test before suggesting a fix.

```
438	func collectObjects(
439		store storer.EncodedObjectStorer,
440		hash plumbing.Hash,
441		haves map[plumbing.Hash]struct{},
442		seen map[plumbing.Hash]bool,
443		out *[]plumbing.Hash,
444	) error {
445		if hash.IsZero() {
446			return nil
447		}
448		if _, ok := haves[hash]; ok {
449			return nil
450		}
451		if seen[hash] {
452			return nil
453		}
454		seen[hash] = true
455	
456		obj, err := store.EncodedObject(plumbing.AnyObject, hash)
457		if err != nil {
458			return fmt.Errorf("load object %s: %w", hash, err)
459		}
460	
461		switch obj.Type() {
462		case plumbing.CommitObject:
463			commit, err := object.GetCommit(store, hash)
464			if err != nil {
465				return fmt.Errorf("load commit %s: %w", hash, err)
466			}
467			if err := collectObjects(store, commit.TreeHash, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
468				return err
469			}
470			for _, ph := range commit.ParentHashes {
471				if err := collectObjects(store, ph, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
472					return err
473				}
474			}
475		case plumbing.TreeObject:
476			tree, err := object.GetTree(store, hash)
477			if err != nil {
478				return fmt.Errorf("load tree %s: %w", hash, err)
479			}
480			for _, entry := range tree.Entries {
481				if err := collectObjects(store, entry.Hash, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
482					return err
483				}
484			}
485		case plumbing.TagObject:
486			tag, err := object.GetTag(store, hash)
487			if err != nil {
488				return fmt.Errorf("load tag %s: %w", hash, err)
489			}
490			if err := collectObjects(store, tag.Target, haves, seen, out); err != nil {
491				return err
492			}
493		case plumbing.BlobObject:
494			// Blobs are leaf objects — nothing to recurse into.
495		case plumbing.InvalidObject, plumbing.OFSDeltaObject, plumbing.REFDeltaObject, plumbing.AnyObject:
496			return fmt.Errorf("unsupported object type %s for %s", obj.Type(), hash)
497		}
498	
499		*out = append(*out, hash)
500		return nil
501	}
502
```

Found a precedent: `isAncestor`'s walker (line 398) already treats `ErrObjectNotFound` as a boundary rather than an error. The `collectObjects` walker doesn't, but should — by the same logic.

Let me write a test that reproduces the bug, then fix it.

```json
  "new_string": "// ObjectsToPush computes the set of objects that need to be sent to the target.\n//\n// Wants must be present in the store; this is verified up front so a missing\n// want fails loudly rather than silently producing an empty pack. Transitive\n// references encountered during the walk are allowed to be missing: a fetch\n// with target refs as haves prunes the closure server-side, so any object\n// reachable from a have is intentionally absent from the local store. Such\n// objects are treated as implicitly have'd by the target and excluded from\n// the push pack.\nfunc ObjectsToPush(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, wants []plumbing.Hash, targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\thaveSet := make(map[plumbing.Hash]struct{})\n\tfor _, h := range targetRefs {\n\t\tif !h.IsZero() {\n\t\t\thaveSet[h] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tfilteredWants := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(wants))\n\tfor _, h := range wants {\n\t\tif _, ok := haveSet[h]; !ok {\n\t\t\tfilteredWants = append(filteredWants, h)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif len(filteredWants) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, h := range filteredWants {\n\t\tif _, err := store.EncodedObject(plumbing.AnyObject, h); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"load want %s: %w\", h, err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tseen := make(map[plumbing.Hash]bool, len(filteredWants)*4)\n\tobjects := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(filteredWants)*16)\n\tfor _, h := range filteredWants {\n\t\tif err := collectObjects(store, h, haveSet, seen, &objects); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn objects, nil\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Full suite is green. Summary of the fix:

## What was wrong

`internal/planner/planner.go:438` (`collectObjects`) walks the source object graph using *only the literal target ref hashes* as haves. But `internal/gitproto/fetch.go:166` sends those same hashes to the source server as `have` lines, and the server prunes everything reachable from them — including blobs and trees that are *transitively* reachable from a have but aren't themselves haves.

Net effect: when the target has an existing ref whose object closure overlaps with the source closure, the walker hits objects the server intentionally didn't send and crashes with `load object <hash>: object not found`. Exactly your symptom.

## The fix (`internal/planner/planner.go`)

1. **`ObjectsToPush`**: validate each filtered want is in the store up front, so a missing top-level want still fails loudly with `load want <hash>: …`.
2. **`collectObjects`**: when `store.EncodedObject` returns `plumbing.ErrObjectNotFound` for a transitively-walked hash, treat it as implicitly have'd (return nil, exclude from the push pack). Other errors still propagate.

Same precedent already exists in `isAncestor` at `planner.go:398` — missing object during the commit walk = boundary, not error.

Why this is safe: if the source server is correct, the only reason a referenced object isn't in our store is that it was reachable from a have we advertised — which means the target already has it, so leaving it out of our push pack is the right answer. If the source server is buggy and produces a real gap, the target's `receive-pack` rejects with "missing necessary objects" — fail-closed at the protocol level.

## Tests added (`internal/planner/planner_test.go`)

- `TestObjectsToPushTransitiveMissing` — builds a want commit whose tree references a blob that's not in the store (simulating the server's prune), with a have ref. Without the fix it produces the exact `load object …: object not found` error you saw; with the fix the pack contains the want commit and its tree, and the missing blob is correctly excluded.
- `TestObjectsToPushMissingWant` — asserts a missing top-level want still surfaces a `load want` error, so we don't silently produce empty packs.

## Files changed

- `internal/gitproto/smarthttp.go` — earlier patch: include diagnostic headers (`Cf-Ray`, `X-Request-Id`, `Server`, `Content-Type`, etc.) in `httpError`, trim trailing whitespace from body.
- `internal/planner/planner.go` — the materialized-walker fix above.
- `internal/planner/planner_test.go` — two new tests.

To validate against your artifacts target: re-run your original `go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --tags https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE`. The materialized fallback path should now succeed if it was the only blocker; if you still hit the 500, the new diagnostic headers in the error message should give you something concrete (`Cf-Ray=…`) to share with Cloudflare.

```
251	func storeV2FetchPack(store storer.Storer, r io.Reader, verbose bool) error {
252		reader := NewPacketReader(r)
253		expectPackfile := false
254		for {
255			kind, payload, err := reader.ReadPacket()
256			if err != nil {
257				if errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
258					if expectPackfile {
259						return errors.New("expected packfile to be sent after 'ready'")
260					}
261					return nil
262				}
263				return fmt.Errorf("decode protocol v2 fetch response: %w", err)
264			}
265			switch kind {
266			case PacketFlush:
267				if expectPackfile {
268					return errors.New("expected packfile to be sent after 'ready'")
269				}
270				return nil
271			case PacketDelim, PacketResponseEnd:
272				continue
273			case PacketData:
274				line := string(payload)
275				if strings.HasPrefix(line, "ERR ") {
276					return errors.New("remote: " + line[4:])
277				}
278				switch line {
279				case "packfile\n":
280					demux := sideband.NewDemuxer(sideband.Sideband64k, reader.BufReader())
281					demux.Progress = progressSink(verbose, "source: ")
282					if err := packfile.UpdateObjectStorage(store, demux); err != nil {
283						return fmt.Errorf("update object storage: %w", err)
284					}
285					return nil
286				case "acknowledgments\n":
287					ready, err := skipV2Acknowledgments(reader)
288					if err != nil {
289						return err
290					}
291					expectPackfile = ready
292				case "shallow-info\n":
293					expectPackfile = true
294					if err := SkipSection(reader); err != nil {
295						return err
296					}
297				default:
298					return fmt.Errorf("unexpected protocol v2 fetch section %q", strings.TrimSpace(line))
299				}
300			}
301		}
302	}
303
```

```
89	// AdvRefsToSlice converts an AdvRefs to a slice of references.
90	func AdvRefsToSlice(ar *packp.AdvRefs) ([]*plumbing.Reference, error) {
91		refs, err := ar.AllReferences()
92		if err != nil {
93			return nil, fmt.Errorf("all references: %w", err)
94		}
95		iter, err := refs.IterReferences()
96		if err != nil {
97			return nil, fmt.Errorf("iter references: %w", err)
98		}
99		defer iter.Close()
100	
101		var out []*plumbing.Reference
102		err = iter.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
103			out = append(out, ref)
104			return nil
105		})
106		if err != nil {
107			return nil, fmt.Errorf("iterate references: %w", err)
108		}
109		return out, nil
110	}

114		if adv == nil || adv.Capabilities == nil {
115			return nil
116		}
117		all := adv.Capabilities.All()
118		items := make([]string, 0, len(all))
119		for _, cap := range all {
120			values := adv.Capabilities.Get(cap)
121			if len(values) == 0 {
122				items = append(items, string(cap))
123				continue
124			}
```

I need to reconsider. The reason the want hash is missing is the same as the transitive case — the source server pruned it because it's reachable from a target have. The target itself has the object (just under a different ref name), so the push will succeed without us needing to include it. Let me confirm this hypothesis and adjust the fix.

> AGENT

Confirmed: `0fccf698` is an ancestor of `main` on cli.git. So:

1. User previously pushed `main` (or a descendant) to the artifacts target, which gives target the object closure of those commits — including `0fccf698`.
2. New sync includes branch `1072-…` at `0fccf698` as a new ref to create.
3. Source server sees `want 0fccf698` and a `have` (target's `main`) that already covers it → server doesn't include it in the pack.
4. Planner builds a Create plan for `1072-…` referencing `0fccf698`.
5. Materialized walker tries to load the want from store and fails.

The push itself would succeed if we just sent the ref command without the object — target already has `0fccf698` reachable from its `main`, so `receive-pack` accepts. The right fix is the same as the transitive case: a missing want is *implicitly* already on target. Let me revise.

```json
  "old_string": "// ObjectsToPush computes the set of objects that need to be sent to the target.\n//\n// Wants must be present in the store; this is verified up front so a missing\n// want fails loudly rather than silently producing an empty pack. Transitive\n// references encountered during the walk are allowed to be missing: a fetch\n// with target refs as haves prunes the closure server-side, so any object\n// reachable from a have is intentionally absent from the local store. Such\n// objects are treated as implicitly have'd by the target and excluded from\n// the push pack.\nfunc ObjectsToPush(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, wants []plumbing.Hash, targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\thaveSet := make(map[plumbing.Hash]struct{})\n\tfor _, h := range targetRefs {\n\t\tif !h.IsZero() {\n\t\t\thaveSet[h] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tfilteredWants := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(wants))\n\tfor _, h := range wants {\n\t\tif _, ok := haveSet[h]; !ok {\n\t\t\tfilteredWants = append(filteredWants, h)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif len(filteredWants) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, h := range filteredWants {\n\t\tif _, err := store.EncodedObject(plumbing.AnyObject, h); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"load want %s: %w\", h, err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tseen := make(map[plumbing.Hash]bool, len(filteredWants)*4)\n\tobjects := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(filteredWants)*16)\n\tfor _, h := range filteredWants {\n\t\tif err := collectObjects(store, h, haveSet, seen, &objects); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn objects, nil\n}",
  "new_string": "// ObjectsToPush computes the set of objects that need to be sent to the target.\n//\n// A fetch with target refs as haves prunes the source pack server-side, so\n// objects reachable from a have are intentionally absent from the local\n// store. Both top-level wants and transitive references encountered during\n// the walk may be missing for this reason — they're treated as implicitly\n// have'd by the target and excluded from the push pack. The target's\n// receive-pack accepts ref updates referencing such objects because it\n// already has them under one of its existing refs.\nfunc ObjectsToPush(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, wants []plumbing.Hash, targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) ([]plumbing.Hash, error) {\n\thaveSet := make(map[plumbing.Hash]struct{})\n\tfor _, h := range targetRefs {\n\t\tif !h.IsZero() {\n\t\t\thaveSet[h] = struct{}{}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tfilteredWants := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(wants))\n\tfor _, h := range wants {\n\t\tif _, ok := haveSet[h]; !ok {\n\t\t\tfilteredWants = append(filteredWants, h)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif len(filteredWants) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tseen := make(map[plumbing.Hash]bool, len(filteredWants)*4)\n\tobjects := make([]plumbing.Hash, 0, len(filteredWants)*16)\n\tfor _, h := range filteredWants {\n\t\tif err := collectObjects(store, h, haveSet, seen, &objects); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn objects, nil\n}"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "// TestObjectsToPushMissingWant verifies a missing want still surfaces a\n// loud error — we only tolerate missing transitive refs, not missing\n// top-level wants.\nfunc TestObjectsToPushMissingWant(t *testing.T) {\n\trepo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage(), nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"init repo: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tmissing := plumbing.NewHash(\"cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc\")\n\t_, err = ObjectsToPush(repo.Storer, []plumbing.Hash{missing}, nil)\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"expected error for missing want, got nil\")\n\t}\n\tif !strings.Contains(err.Error(), \"load want\") {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"expected error to mention 'load want', got: %v\", err)\n\t}\n}",
  "new_string": "// TestObjectsToPushMissingWantImplicitlyHaved covers the case where the\n// source server pruned a top-level want because it's reachable from a\n// target have under a different ref name (e.g. source branch X is at a\n// commit that already lies in target's main history). The walker must\n// treat the want as implicitly have'd: the target's receive-pack accepts\n// the ref update because it already has the object.\n//\n// Without this tolerance the materialized fallback fails with\n// \"load want <hash>: object not found\" even though the push would succeed.\nfunc TestObjectsToPushMissingWantImplicitlyHaved(t *testing.T) {\n\trepo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage(), nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"init repo: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\t// Source's \"1072-...\" branch tip — server pruned it because target's\n\t// main (a have) already reaches this commit. Not in our store.\n\tprunedWant := plumbing.NewHash(\"cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc\")\n\t// Target's main, the have that caused the prune.\n\ttargetMain := plumbing.NewHash(\"dddddddddddddddddddddddddddddddddddddddd\")\n\ttargetRefs := map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash{\n\t\tplumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(\"main\"): targetMain,\n\t}\n\thashes, err := ObjectsToPush(repo.Storer, []plumbing.Hash{prunedWant}, targetRefs)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"ObjectsToPush: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tfor _, h := range hashes {\n\t\tif h == prunedWant {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"pack includes pruned want %s; should be excluded\", h)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

All green. Try your sync command again — it should get past the materialized walker now. If the pack ends up empty for a given branch (target already has the object), the receive-pack push will still send the ref-update command and target will accept it because it has the object reachable from one of its existing refs.

Quick recap of the revised diagnosis:

`0fccf698…` is the tip of `1072-…` on cli.git, and it's an ancestor of `main`. Your earlier push put `main` (or a descendant) on the artifacts target, so the target now reachably has `0fccf698`. When this sync ran, the source server saw `want 0fccf698` and `have <target's main>`, decided the client already had the closure, and pruned it from the pack. The walker then couldn't load the want.

The fix in `internal/planner/planner.go`: drop the up-front "wants must be in store" check — both top-level wants and transitive references can legitimately be missing for the same reason (server pruned them as reachable from a have). The walker now treats any `ErrObjectNotFound` as an implicit-have boundary. Updated the test to cover this case (`TestObjectsToPushMissingWantImplicitlyHaved`), kept the transitive-missing test, removed the now-incorrect "missing want errors loudly" test.

If you do hit a real bad-input case (typo'd hash with no corresponding object on either side), the target's `receive-pack` will reject with "missing necessary objects" — the failure surface moves from the local walker to the target protocol, which is a less local error but still fail-closed.

exit status 1

> AGENT

You've cleared the materialized walker — the error is now from the actual push round-trip. Let me unpack what's going on.

The Go HTTP/2 client received a `PROTOCOL_ERROR` stream reset from the server (Cloudflare). It tried to retry, couldn't because the request body had already been streamed and isn't seekable, and surfaced this composite error. The "real" error is the `stream ID 3; PROTOCOL_ERROR; received from peer` part — Cloudflare's edge sent a `RST_STREAM` mid-push.

That's not a status code, so my earlier diagnostic-headers patch can't help — there's no response body for it to dig into. We never reached the HTTP layer in the way `httpError` runs.

> AGENT

The fastest way to see what's actually wrong is to force HTTP/1.1 — that bypasses the protocol error and the Go runtime's retry-mask, and the server's real error response (if any) will land in our 500-handler with the new diagnostic headers.

Try:

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --tags https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

(Older Go versions used `GODEBUG=http2disable=1`; `http2client=0` is the current spelling.)

That tells the Go runtime to skip ALPN HTTP/2 negotiation. Three possible outcomes:

1. **The push succeeds** — Cloudflare's HTTP/2 edge had a bug or hit a frame-size limit. We'd want to harden git-sync's transport (cap HTTP/2 use for `receive-pack` or expose a flag).
2. **The push fails with a real HTTP error** — `httpError` will render the status, body, and the diagnostic headers (`Cf-Ray=…`) you can hand to whoever runs the artifacts service.
3. **The push fails the same way** — confirms the issue is upstream, not protocol-version-specific.

If you want, I can also add a `--target-disable-http2` flag (or env var) that calls `tc.ForceAttemptHTTP2 = false` and clears `tc.TLSNextProto` on the cloned transport — so this is a one-flag toggle rather than a process-level `GODEBUG`. Worth it once we know which of (1) / (2) / (3) you're in. Run with `GODEBUG=http2client=0` first and report back.

Speculation about the root cause while you try that: HTTP/2 PROTOCOL_ERROR mid-push from a Cloudflare-fronted git endpoint is most often either (a) a backend git process crashed or sent malformed sideband and Cloudflare translated it into a stream reset, or (b) trailers/length mismatch on a chunked body. Both are server-side; neither is something our diagnostic headers patch can recover, but `Cf-Ray` from a successful HTTP/1.1 attempt (or an HTTP/1.1 5xx with a body) gives them the lookup key.

exit status 1

> AGENT

Same family of failure as the HTTP/2 PROTOCOL_ERROR — the server (or Cloudflare's edge) is closing the connection mid-push, before we ever get an HTTP response back. With HTTP/1.1 the symptom changes from a frame-level reset to a TCP-level close, but both mean the same thing: the target is hanging up while we're streaming the pack.

So `httpError` and the diagnostic headers can't help here either — there's no response to inspect. We're getting cut off before the server commits to one.

A couple of things worth trying, in order of effort:

**1. Try a vanilla `git push` to the same target.** If git itself fails with a clear server-side error, the artifacts service has a real bug and your reproduction is just confirming it. If vanilla git succeeds, then git-sync is sending something the server rejects.

```sh
git clone --bare https://github.com/entireio/cli.git /tmp/cli.git
cd /tmp/cli.git
git push --mirror $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

**2. Run git-sync with `--verbose`** so the server's sideband progress (if any reaches us before the close) is printed. Worth a shot:

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --tags --verbose \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

**3. Check the artifacts server logs.** If you can get to whatever's serving `8b04592e…artifacts.cloudflare.net`, the corresponding request will be there. The original 500 you saw earlier in this thread, this PROTOCOL_ERROR, and now this connection-close are all server-emitted — server-side logs are where the real story lives.

**4. Try `--target-max-pack-bytes`** to send a smaller pack and see if size is the trigger:

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --target-max-pack-bytes 10485760 \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

(10 MiB. Drop to 1 MiB if it still fails.)

Quick sanity check on my hypothesis from the previous turn — that target already has all the objects via main. Worth verifying:

> AGENT

`$ARTIFACTS_REMOTE` isn't set in my shell, so I can't probe the target. But you can — running `git ls-remote --refs $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE` will show exactly which refs are already on the target. That'd confirm whether my "target already has main reachable from this object" hypothesis explains the materialized-walker case from the previous turn (separate from this current connection-close issue).

For the connection-close itself, the actionable next step is **option 1 above**: do a vanilla `git push --mirror` from a fresh bare clone of cli.git to the same artifacts URL. The outcomes:

- **Vanilla git push fails too** → artifacts server has a real issue with cli.git (possibly large-pack handling, slow client, auth quirk, or genuine bug). Not a git-sync bug. Server-side investigation only.
- **Vanilla git push succeeds** → git-sync is producing a request the server rejects. Most likely culprit given the path we're on: the materialized strategy is now successfully producing a pack but it's a *thin* pack (delta bases reference objects on the target that aren't in our store/pack), and Cloudflare's git backend may not accept thin packs even though the protocol allows them. Worth investigating.

Either way, the connection close + PROTOCOL_ERROR pattern is server-emitted and there's no client-side fix that would surface a clearer error than what we have. Try the vanilla push first; the answer dictates where to look next.

> AGENT

Confirmed — target has `main` plus the v0.x tags, source has all that plus 50+ branches. So git-sync correctly identifies a lot of branch creations to push. With my walker fix, every want hash that's reachable from `main` (which is most of these branches, since they were merged into main) gets pruned by the source server and skipped by the walker. That part is working as intended.

The actual push to receive-pack is what's failing — and the failure mode (PROTOCOL_ERROR / connection close, no HTTP body) is server-emitted with no client-side recovery available. Three concrete experiments to narrow it down, in order:

**A. Push *just* main (a no-op) to verify the protocol/auth path works at all**

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --branches main \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

This should be near-instant (main is already at the right hash). If it succeeds, the auth and basic push work. If it fails the same way, the issue isn't pack content — it's something about the request itself.

**B. Push a single new branch at a time to isolate**

Pick one branch the target doesn't have, e.g.:

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync \
  --branches 1072-intermittent-stop-hook-timeout-after-30s-on-windows-with-concurrent-codexclaude-sessions \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

That branch's tip (`0fccf698`) is reachable from main, so the pack for that push should be effectively empty (just a ref-update command). If the server rejects an empty-pack ref-create, that's a strong signal the artifacts service has a specific bug.

**C. Vanilla git push for the same single branch**

```sh
git -C /tmp/cli-probe push $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE \
  refs/heads/1072-intermittent-stop-hook-timeout-after-30s-on-windows-with-concurrent-codexclaude-sessions
```

Same operation, vanilla git. If C succeeds and B fails, git-sync is sending something subtly different. If both fail, server bug.

The most informative comparison is B vs C with the same single ref. That'll cleanly answer whether this is a git-sync issue or an artifacts-service issue, without confounding factors from large packs or many refs.

> AGENT

So the bottleneck is specifically the materialized fallback when many refs are pushed together. The single-branch case (B) won the bootstrap path because once you scoped to one branch, target had zero managed refs → bootstrap relay → success.

Why does the full sync end up in materialized? Because target already has `main` (a managed ref name), bootstrap is skipped. Then incremental relay is tried — and presumably gives up for some reason — falling back to materialized.

```
1	// Package incremental implements the incremental relay strategy for git-sync.
2	// This fast-path streams a pack from source directly to target when all updates
3	// are fast-forward branch updates or new tag creates.
4	package incremental
5	
6	import (
7		"context"
8		"errors"
9		"fmt"
10		"io"
11		"sync"
12	
13		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
14	
15		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/convert"
16		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/gitproto"
17		"entire.io/entire/git-sync/internal/planner"
18	)
19	
20	// Params holds the inputs for an incremental relay execution.
21	type Params struct {
22		SourceConn    *gitproto.Conn
23		SourceService interface {
24			FetchPack(ctx context.Context, conn *gitproto.Conn, desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef, haves map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash) (io.ReadCloser, error)
25		}
26		TargetPusher interface {
27			PushPack(ctx context.Context, cmds []gitproto.PushCommand, pack io.ReadCloser) error
28		}
29		DesiredRefs  map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef
30		TargetRefs   map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash
31		PushPlans    []planner.BranchPlan
32		MaxPackBytes int64
33		Verbose      bool
34		CanRelay     func(bool, bool, bool, []planner.BranchPlan) (bool, string)
35		CanTagRelay  func([]planner.BranchPlan) (bool, string)
36	}
37	
38	// Result holds the outcome of an incremental relay.
39	type Result struct {
40		Relay       bool
41		RelayMode   string
42		RelayReason string
43	}
44	
45	// Execute attempts the incremental relay strategy. Returns (result, nil) on
46	// success, or (zero, nil) if the strategy is not applicable. Errors indicate
47	// a relay was attempted but failed.
48	func Execute(ctx context.Context, p Params, cfg planner.PlanConfig) (Result, error) {
49		canRelay := p.CanRelay
50		if canRelay == nil {
51			return Result{}, errors.New("incremental strategy requires CanRelay")
52		}
53		if p.TargetPusher == nil {
54			return Result{}, errors.New("incremental strategy requires TargetPusher")
55		}
56		cmds := convert.PlansToPushCommands(p.PushPlans)
57		if ok, reason := canRelay(cfg.Force, cfg.Prune, false, p.PushPlans); ok {
58			desired := convert.DesiredRefsForPlans(p.DesiredRefs, p.PushPlans)
59			packReader, err := p.SourceService.FetchPack(ctx, p.SourceConn, desired, p.TargetRefs)
60			if err != nil {
61				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("fetch source pack: %w", err)
62			}
63			packReader = gitproto.LimitPackReader(packReader, p.MaxPackBytes)
64			packReader = closeOnce(packReader)
65			if err := p.TargetPusher.PushPack(ctx, cmds, packReader); err != nil {
66				_ = packReader.Close()
67				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
68			}
69			_ = packReader.Close()
70			return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "incremental", RelayReason: reason}, nil
71		}
72	
73		if p.CanTagRelay == nil {
74			return Result{}, errors.New("incremental strategy requires CanTagRelay")
75		}
76		if ok, reason := p.CanTagRelay(p.PushPlans); ok {
77			desired := convert.DesiredRefsForPlans(p.DesiredRefs, p.PushPlans)
78			packReader, err := p.SourceService.FetchPack(ctx, p.SourceConn, desired, nil)
79			if err != nil {
80				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("fetch source tag pack: %w", err)
81			}
82			packReader = gitproto.LimitPackReader(packReader, p.MaxPackBytes)
83			packReader = closeOnce(packReader)
84			if err := p.TargetPusher.PushPack(ctx, cmds, packReader); err != nil {
85				_ = packReader.Close()
86				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("push target refs: %w", err)
87			}
88			_ = packReader.Close()
89			return Result{Relay: true, RelayMode: "incremental", RelayReason: reason}, nil
90		}
91	
92		return Result{}, nil
93	}
94	
95	type closeOnceReadCloser struct {
96		io.ReadCloser
97	
98		once sync.Once
99	}
100	
101	func (c *closeOnceReadCloser) Close() error {
102		var err error
103		c.once.Do(func() {
104			err = c.ReadCloser.Close()
105		})
106		if err != nil {
107			return fmt.Errorf("close pack reader: %w", err)
108		}
109		return nil
110	}
111	
112	func closeOnce(rc io.ReadCloser) io.ReadCloser {
113		if rc == nil {
114			return nil
115		}
116		if _, ok := rc.(*closeOnceReadCloser); ok {
117			return rc
118		}
119		return &closeOnceReadCloser{ReadCloser: rc}
120	}
121
```

```
55	// CanIncrementalRelay checks whether all plans are eligible for the incremental
56	// relay fast-path (fast-forward branch updates + new tag creates).
57	func CanIncrementalRelay(force, prune, dryRun bool, plans []BranchPlan, target RelayTargetPolicy) (bool, string) {
58		if force || prune || dryRun {
59			return false, "incremental-disabled-by-force-prune-or-dry-run"

61		if len(plans) == 0 {

63		}
64		if !target.CapabilitiesKnown {
65			return false, "incremental-missing-target-capabilities"
66		}
67		if target.NoThin {
68			return false, "incremental-target-no-thin"
69		}
70	
71		for _, plan := range plans {
72			switch plan.Kind {
73			case RefKindBranch:
74				if !plan.SourceRef.IsBranch() || !plan.TargetRef.IsBranch() {
75					return false, "incremental-non-branch-mapping"
76				}
77				if plan.Action != ActionUpdate {
78					return false, "incremental-branch-action-not-update"
79				}
80				if plan.TargetHash.IsZero() {
81					return false, "incremental-branch-target-missing"
82				}
83			case RefKindTag:
84				if !plan.SourceRef.IsTag() || !plan.TargetRef.IsTag() {
85					return false, "incremental-non-tag-mapping"
86				}
87				if plan.Action != ActionCreate {
88					return false, "incremental-tag-action-not-create"
89				}
90			default:
91				return false, "incremental-unsupported-ref-kind"
92			}
93		}
94		return true, "fast-forward-branch-or-tag-create"
95	}
96
```

> AGENT

Found the relay-decision wrinkle. Looking at `internal/planner/relay.go:77`:

```go
case RefKindBranch:
    ...
    if plan.Action != ActionUpdate {
        return false, "incremental-branch-action-not-update"
    }
```

Incremental relay only accepts `ActionUpdate` (fast-forward) for branches. Your full sync has many `ActionCreate` plans (all the branches target doesn't have yet) → relay rejected → materialized fallback → server rejects the resulting pack for some reason we can't see from the client.

The bootstrap path you saw in test B works exactly because it's branch creates with no other managed refs on target. The relay decision wrote off the multi-create case as "use materialized," and now we know that path doesn't survive the artifacts server.

## Pragmatic workaround now

Push each branch in its own invocation so each one takes the bootstrap path:

```sh
git -C /tmp/cli-probe for-each-ref --format='%(refname:short)' refs/heads/ \
  | grep -v '^main$' \
  | while read branch; do
      echo "=== $branch ==="
      GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --branch "$branch" \
        https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
    done
```

(Slow because it'll ask for credentials per call — pre-cache them with `git config --global credential.helper osxkeychain` or similar before running.) Then a final pass with `--tags` for tag refs.

## Real fix (the actual bug behind the bug)

The relay rule is overly conservative. For `ActionCreate` on a branch where target isn't empty, we *could* still relay: pass all target refs as haves to the source's `upload-pack`. The source emits a minimal pack covering only what target doesn't already have, and the target's `receive-pack` accepts the create command because its closure is reachable from existing target refs — same logic as the implicit-have fix we just landed in the materialized walker.

That'd make incremental relay handle "create branch" alongside "update branch," which is the common case for syncing a new mirror to an artifacts service that already has `main`. Materialized would only kick in for genuinely tricky cases (force, prune, retargets).

Worth doing as a follow-up — it's a bigger change than today's fix and needs careful testing for the relay invariants. Want me to draft it as a separate change, or stop here for now?

For your immediate goal of validating "is sync working," the per-branch loop above should let you mirror cli.git successfully and confirm git-sync's logic end-to-end against the artifacts target.

```
152	func adjustedBootstrapTargetRefs(
153		desiredRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef,
154		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
155	) map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash {
156		if len(targetRefs) == 0 {
157			return targetRefs
158		}
159		adjusted := planner.CopyRefHashMap(targetRefs)
160		for targetRef, desired := range desiredRefs {
161			if desired.Kind != planner.RefKindBranch {
162				continue
163			}
164			tempRef := planner.BootstrapTempRef(targetRef)
165			if adjusted[targetRef] == desired.SourceHash && adjusted[tempRef] == desired.SourceHash {
166				adjusted[targetRef] = plumbing.ZeroHash
167			}
168		}
169		return adjusted
170	}
171	
172	// --- Batched bootstrap ---
173	
174	func executeBatched( //nolint:maintidx // complex batch logic is inherently branchy
175		ctx context.Context,
176		p Params,
177		plans []planner.BranchPlan,
178		result Result,
179	) (Result, error) {
180		if !p.SourceService.SupportsBootstrapBatch() {
181			return result, errors.New("bootstrap batching requires protocol v2 source fetch filter support")
182		}
183	
184		planRefs := make([]planner.DesiredRef, 0, len(plans))
185		tagPlans := make([]planner.BranchPlan, 0, len(plans))
186		tagDesired := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]gitproto.DesiredRef)
187		for _, plan := range plans {
188			if plan.Kind == planner.RefKindTag {
189				tagPlans = append(tagPlans, plan)
190				if d, ok := p.DesiredRefs[plan.TargetRef]; ok {
191					tagDesired[plan.TargetRef] = gitproto.DesiredRef{
192						SourceRef: d.SourceRef, TargetRef: d.TargetRef,
193						SourceHash: d.SourceHash, IsTag: true,
194					}
195				}
196				continue
197			}
198			if !plan.SourceRef.IsBranch() || !plan.TargetRef.IsBranch() {
199				return result, errors.New("bootstrap batching currently supports branch refs and create-only tags")
200			}
201			planRefs = append(planRefs, p.DesiredRefs[plan.TargetRef])
202		}
203	
204		var batches []plannedBatch
205		if len(planRefs) > 0 {
206			p.log("bootstrap batch planning checkpoints", "branch_ref_count", len(planRefs))
207			var err error
208			batches, err = planBatches(ctx, p, planRefs)
209			if err != nil {
210				return result, err
211			}
212		}
213	
214		// MaxPackBytes is the hard abort threshold for any single source fetch.
215		// TargetMaxPack controls checkpoint *placement* (how many batches) but
216		// should not cap individual fetches — the estimate may undercount, and
217		// the actual pack for a batch can legitimately exceed the planning
218		// heuristic. If the resulting pack is too large for the target's
219		// receive-pack, the push itself fails and resume handles retry.
220		fetchLimit := p.MaxPackBytes
221		// Track branch tips already pushed to the target so subsequent branches
222		// can advertise them as haves. Without this, the second branch in a
223		// multi-branch bootstrap re-sends all shared objects (e.g., linux's
224		// master and nocache-cleanup share ~99% of history).
225		completedRefs := planner.CopyRefHashMap(p.TargetRefs)
226	
227		for _, batch := range batches {
228			if batch.subsumed {
229				cmds := []gitproto.PushCommand{{
230					Name: batch.Plan.TargetRef,
231					Old:  plumbing.ZeroHash,
232					New:  batch.Plan.SourceHash,
233				}}
234				if err := p.TargetPusher.PushCommands(ctx, cmds); err != nil {
235					return result, fmt.Errorf("create subsumed branch ref for %s: %w", batch.Plan.TargetRef, err)
236				}
237				completedRefs[batch.Plan.TargetRef] = batch.Plan.SourceHash
238				result.BatchCount++
239				p.log("bootstrap batch subsumed branch finalized",
240					"branch", batch.Plan.TargetRef.String(),
241					"source_hash", planner.ShortHash(batch.Plan.SourceHash))
242				continue
243			}
244			result.PlannedBatchCount += len(batch.Checkpoints)
245			result.TempRefs = append(result.TempRefs, batch.TempRef.String())
246			p.log("bootstrap batch branch plan",
247				"branch", batch.Plan.TargetRef.String(),
248				"temp_ref", batch.TempRef.String(),
249				"planned_batches", len(batch.Checkpoints),
250				"resume_hash", planner.ShortHash(batch.ResumeHash))
251
```

```
1	package planner

3	import (
4		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"
5	)
6	
7	// RelayTargetPolicy captures only the target-side facts that affect planner
8	// relay eligibility decisions.
9	type RelayTargetPolicy struct {
10		CapabilitiesKnown bool
11		NoThin            bool
12	}
13	
14	// SupportsReplicateRelay checks target-side capabilities required by
15	// replication mode before looking at planned ref actions.
16	//
17	// Replicate tolerates targets that advertise "no-thin" because our
18	// upload-pack client (gitproto.FetchPack) never requests the "thin-pack"
19	// capability, so the source never emits a thin pack. The relayed pack is
20	// always self-contained and safe to push to a no-thin receive-pack.
21	// If gitproto.FetchPack ever begins requesting thin-pack, this check must
22	// gain a matching fallback (omit the request, or explicitly advertise
23	// NoThin on the source request) when target.NoThin is set.
24	func SupportsReplicateRelay(target RelayTargetPolicy) (bool, string) {
25		if !target.CapabilitiesKnown {
26			return false, "replicate-missing-target-capabilities"
27		}
28		if target.NoThin {
29			return true, "replicate-target-capable-no-thin"
30		}
31		return true, "replicate-target-capable"
32	}
33	
34	// CanBootstrapRelay checks whether all desired target refs are absent on the target,
35	// making a bootstrap relay possible.
36	func CanBootstrapRelay(
37		force, prune bool,
38		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef,
39		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
40	) (bool, string) {
41		if force || prune {
42			return false, "bootstrap-disabled-by-force-or-prune"
43		}
44		if len(desired) == 0 {
45			return false, "bootstrap-no-managed-refs"
46		}
47		for targetRef := range desired {
48			if !targetRefs[targetRef].IsZero() {
49				return false, "bootstrap-target-ref-exists"
50			}
51		}
52		return true, "empty-target-managed-refs"
53	}
```

```
304	func openV2PackStream(body io.ReadCloser, verbose bool) (io.ReadCloser, error) {
305		reader := NewPacketReader(body)
306		for {
307			kind, payload, err := reader.ReadPacket()
308			if err != nil {
309				if errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
310					return nil, io.ErrUnexpectedEOF
311				}
312				return nil, fmt.Errorf("decode protocol v2 fetch response: %w", err)
313			}
314			switch kind {
315			case PacketFlush:
316				return nil, io.ErrUnexpectedEOF
317			case PacketDelim, PacketResponseEnd:
318				continue
319			case PacketData:
320				line := string(payload)
321				if strings.HasPrefix(line, "ERR ") {
322					return nil, errors.New("remote: " + line[4:])
323				}
324				switch line {
325				case "packfile\n":
326					demux := sideband.NewDemuxer(sideband.Sideband64k, reader.BufReader())
327					demux.Progress = progressSink(verbose, "source: ")
328					return &wrappedRC{
329						Reader: demux,
330						Closer: body,
331					}, nil
332				case "acknowledgments\n":
333					if _, err := skipV2Acknowledgments(reader); err != nil {
334						return nil, err
335					}
336				case "shallow-info\n":
337					if err := SkipSection(reader); err != nil {
338						return nil, err
339					}
340				default:
341					return nil, fmt.Errorf("unexpected protocol v2 fetch section %q", strings.TrimSpace(line))
342				}
343			}
344		}
345	}
346	
347	func skipV2Acknowledgments(reader *PacketReader) (bool, error) {
348		ready := false
349		for {
350			kind, payload, err := reader.ReadPacket()
351			if err != nil {
352				return false, err
353			}
354			switch kind {
355			case PacketFlush:
356				if ready {
357					return false, errors.New("expected packfile to be sent after 'ready'")
358				}
359				return false, errors.New("protocol v2 fetch response ended without packfile after acknowledgments")
360			case PacketDelim:
361				if !ready {
362					return false, errors.New("unexpected delimiter in acknowledgments section without 'ready'")
363				}
364				return true, nil
365			case PacketData:
366				line := string(payload)
367				switch {
368				case line == "NAK\n", strings.HasPrefix(line, "ACK "):
369					continue
370				case line == "ready\n":
371					ready = true
372				default:
373					return false, fmt.Errorf("unexpected acknowledgment line %q", strings.TrimSpace(line))
374				}
375			case PacketResponseEnd:
376				return false, fmt.Errorf("unexpected packet type %v in acknowledgments section", kind)
377			default:
378				return false, fmt.Errorf("unexpected packet type %v in acknowledgments section", kind)
379			}
380		}
381	}
382

384
```
