```
386	func buildV1UploadPackBody(
387		adv *packp.AdvRefs,
388		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef,
389		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
390		includeTags bool,
391		verbose bool,
392	) ([]byte, *capability.List, error) {

394		haves := SortedUniqueHashes(refValues(targetRefs))
395		if len(wants) == 0 {
396			return nil, nil, git.NoErrAlreadyUpToDate
397		}
398	
399		req := packp.NewUploadRequest()
```

```json
  "old_string": "// CanIncrementalRelay checks whether all plans are eligible for the incremental\n// relay fast-path (fast-forward branch updates + new tag creates).\nfunc CanIncrementalRelay(force, prune, dryRun bool, plans []BranchPlan, target RelayTargetPolicy) (bool, string) {\n\tif force || prune || dryRun {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-disabled-by-force-prune-or-dry-run\"\n\t}\n\tif len(plans) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-no-plans\"\n\t}\n\tif !target.CapabilitiesKnown {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-missing-target-capabilities\"\n\t}\n\tif target.NoThin {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-target-no-thin\"\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, plan := range plans {\n\t\tswitch plan.Kind {\n\t\tcase RefKindBranch:\n\t\t\tif !plan.SourceRef.IsBranch() || !plan.TargetRef.IsBranch() {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-non-branch-mapping\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif plan.Action != ActionUpdate {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-branch-action-not-update\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif plan.TargetHash.IsZero() {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-branch-target-missing\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tcase RefKindTag:\n\t\t\tif !plan.SourceRef.IsTag() || !plan.TargetRef.IsTag() {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-non-tag-mapping\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif plan.Action != ActionCreate {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-tag-action-not-create\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-unsupported-ref-kind\"\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn true, \"fast-forward-branch-or-tag-create\"\n}",
  "new_string": "// CanIncrementalRelay checks whether all plans are eligible for the incremental\n// relay fast-path. Eligible plan shapes:\n//   - Branch fast-forward update (TargetHash is parent of SourceHash).\n//   - Branch create (target has no such ref yet). The relay still passes\n//     all target refs as haves, so the source pack covers only objects\n//     target doesn't already have via some existing ref. The receive-pack\n//     accepts the create command because any pruned objects are reachable\n//     from those existing refs.\n//   - New tag create.\nfunc CanIncrementalRelay(force, prune, dryRun bool, plans []BranchPlan, target RelayTargetPolicy) (bool, string) {\n\tif force || prune || dryRun {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-disabled-by-force-prune-or-dry-run\"\n\t}\n\tif len(plans) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-no-plans\"\n\t}\n\tif !target.CapabilitiesKnown {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-missing-target-capabilities\"\n\t}\n\tif target.NoThin {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-target-no-thin\"\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, plan := range plans {\n\t\tswitch plan.Kind {\n\t\tcase RefKindBranch:\n\t\t\tif !plan.SourceRef.IsBranch() || !plan.TargetRef.IsBranch() {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-non-branch-mapping\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tswitch plan.Action {\n\t\t\tcase ActionUpdate:\n\t\t\t\tif plan.TargetHash.IsZero() {\n\t\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-branch-target-missing\"\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcase ActionCreate:\n\t\t\t\tif !plan.TargetHash.IsZero() {\n\t\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-branch-create-target-not-empty\"\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-branch-action-not-update-or-create\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tcase RefKindTag:\n\t\t\tif !plan.SourceRef.IsTag() || !plan.TargetRef.IsTag() {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-non-tag-mapping\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif plan.Action != ActionCreate {\n\t\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-tag-action-not-create\"\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\treturn false, \"incremental-unsupported-ref-kind\"\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn true, \"fast-forward-branch-or-tag-create\"\n}"
}
```

```
18	// NewMemoryRepo creates an in-memory repository with a memfs-backed worktree.
19	func NewMemoryRepo(tb testing.TB) (*git.Repository, billy.Filesystem) {
20		tb.Helper()
21	
22		fs := memfs.New()
23		repo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage(), git.WithWorkTree(fs))
24		if err != nil {
25			tb.Fatalf("init repo: %v", err)
26		}
27		return repo, fs
28	}

30	// MakeCommits writes and commits tracked.txt repeatedly on the current branch.
31	func MakeCommits(tb testing.TB, repo *git.Repository, fs billy.Filesystem, count int) {
32		tb.Helper()
33	
34		wt, err := repo.Worktree()
35		if err != nil {
36			tb.Fatalf("open worktree: %v", err)
37		}
38	
39		for i := range count {
40			content := strings.Repeat(fmt.Sprintf("line %d %d\n", i, time.Now().UnixNano()), 24)
41			file, err := fs.Create("tracked.txt")
42			if err != nil {
43				tb.Fatalf("create file: %v", err)
44			}
45			if _, err := io.WriteString(file, content); err != nil {
46				tb.Fatalf("write file: %v", err)
47			}
48			if err := file.Close(); err != nil {
49				tb.Fatalf("close file: %v", err)
50			}
51			if _, err := wt.Add("tracked.txt"); err != nil {
52				tb.Fatalf("add file: %v", err)
53			}
54			if _, err := wt.Commit(fmt.Sprintf("commit %d", i), &git.CommitOptions{
55				Author:    &defaultSignature,
56				Committer: &defaultSignature,
57			}); err != nil {
58				tb.Fatalf("commit: %v", err)
59			}
60		}
61	}

99	func MakeLargeCommits(tb testing.TB, repo *git.Repository, fs billy.Filesystem, count int, blobSize int) {
100		tb.Helper()
101

112			}
113			content := make([]byte, blobSize)

115			for idx := range content {
116				state ^= state << 13
117				state ^= state >> 17
118				state ^= state << 5
119				content[idx] = byte(state >> 24)
120			}
121			if _, err := file.Write(content); err != nil {
122				tb.Fatalf("write file: %v", err)
123			}
124			if err := file.Close(); err != nil {

126			}

133			}); err != nil {

139	// AssertBranchHeadsMatch verifies that two repos agree on the named branch tip.
140	func AssertBranchHeadsMatch(tb testing.TB, sourceRepo, targetRepo *git.Repository, branch string) {
141		tb.Helper()
142	
143		sourceRef, err := sourceRepo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branch), true)
144		if err != nil {
145			tb.Fatalf("resolve source ref: %v", err)
146		}
147		targetRef, err := targetRepo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branch), true)
148		if err != nil {
149			tb.Fatalf("resolve target ref: %v", err)
150		}
151		if sourceRef.Hash() != targetRef.Hash() {
152			tb.Fatalf("branch %s mismatch: source=%s target=%s", branch, sourceRef.Hash(), targetRef.Hash())
153		}
154	}
```

> AGENT

Looking deeper, there's a second over-conservative constraint that prevents the fix from helping in some cases: `CanIncrementalRelay` rejects targets that advertise `no-thin`, even though `gitproto.FetchPack` never requests `thin-pack` capability — so the relayed pack is always self-contained. The replicate path already has this exact rationale documented (`relay.go:14-23`); incremental should follow the same logic.

Let me relax that too, then update the integration test that depended on the restriction.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01PujNnRC6mGpfU75NmPgwrU
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/planner/relay.go",
  "old_string": "// CanIncrementalRelay checks whether all plans are eligible for the incremental\n// relay fast-path. Eligible plan shapes:\n//   - Branch fast-forward update (TargetHash is parent of SourceHash).\n//   - Branch create (target has no such ref yet). The relay still passes\n//     all target refs as haves, so the source pack covers only objects\n//     target doesn't already have via some existing ref. The receive-pack\n//     accepts the create command because any pruned objects are reachable\n//     from those existing refs.\n//   - New tag create.\nfunc CanIncrementalRelay(force, prune, dryRun bool, plans []BranchPlan, target RelayTargetPolicy) (bool, string) {\n\tif force || prune || dryRun {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-disabled-by-force-prune-or-dry-run\"\n\t}\n\tif len(plans) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-no-plans\"\n\t}\n\tif !target.CapabilitiesKnown {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-missing-target-capabilities\"\n\t}\n\tif target.NoThin {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-target-no-thin\"\n\t}",
  "new_string": "// CanIncrementalRelay checks whether all plans are eligible for the incremental\n// relay fast-path. Eligible plan shapes:\n//   - Branch fast-forward update (TargetHash is parent of SourceHash).\n//   - Branch create (target has no such ref yet). The relay still passes\n//     all target refs as haves, so the source pack covers only objects\n//     target doesn't already have via some existing ref. The receive-pack\n//     accepts the create command because any pruned objects are reachable\n//     from those existing refs.\n//   - New tag create.\n//\n// Incremental tolerates targets that advertise \"no-thin\" for the same reason\n// as replicate (see SupportsReplicateRelay): gitproto.FetchPack never requests\n// the \"thin-pack\" capability, so the relayed pack is always self-contained.\n// If gitproto.FetchPack ever begins requesting thin-pack, this function must\n// gain a matching fallback when target.NoThin is set.\nfunc CanIncrementalRelay(force, prune, dryRun bool, plans []BranchPlan, target RelayTargetPolicy) (bool, string) {\n\tif force || prune || dryRun {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-disabled-by-force-prune-or-dry-run\"\n\t}\n\tif len(plans) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-no-plans\"\n\t}\n\tif !target.CapabilitiesKnown {\n\t\treturn false, \"incremental-missing-target-capabilities\"\n\t}"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "func TestCanIncrementalRelayToleratesNoThin(t *testing.T) {\n\t// Incremental relay tolerates \"no-thin\" targets because gitproto.FetchPack\n\t// never requests the thin-pack capability, so the relayed pack is always\n\t// self-contained and safe for no-thin receive-pack servers — same logic\n\t// as SupportsReplicateRelay.\n\tplans := []BranchPlan{{\n\t\tBranch:     \"main\",\n\t\tSourceRef:  \"refs/heads/main\",\n\t\tTargetRef:  \"refs/heads/main\",\n\t\tSourceHash: plumbing.NewHash(\"1111111111111111111111111111111111111111\"),\n\t\tTargetHash: plumbing.NewHash(\"2222222222222222222222222222222222222222\"),\n\t\tKind:       RefKindBranch,\n\t\tAction:     ActionUpdate,\n\t}}\n\n\tok, reason := CanIncrementalRelay(false, false, false, plans, RelayTargetPolicy{CapabilitiesKnown: true, NoThin: true})\n\tif !ok {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected CanIncrementalRelay=true for no-thin target, got reason=%s\", reason)\n\t}\n\tif reason != \"fast-forward-branch-or-tag-create\" {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"unexpected reason: %s\", reason)\n\t}\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

All green. Recap of the proper fix:

## What changed

`internal/planner/relay.go` — `CanIncrementalRelay`:

1. **Branch creates are now relay-eligible.** Previously rejected as `incremental-branch-action-not-update`, even though the relay path already passes target refs as haves and the receive-pack accepts the create when the new ref's closure is reachable from existing target refs. New rejection: only if `Action == ActionCreate` but `TargetHash != ZeroHash` (incoherent plan shape).
2. **`no-thin` targets are now tolerated.** Same rationale as `SupportsReplicateRelay` (`relay.go:14-23`): `gitproto.FetchPack` never requests `thin-pack`, so the relayed pack is always self-contained and safe for any receive-pack. Comment updated to mirror the replicate one — if `FetchPack` ever requests `thin-pack`, both functions will need a matching fallback.

## Tests

`internal/planner/planner_test.go`:
- Replaced `TestCanIncrementalRelayRejectsBranchCreate` with `TestCanIncrementalRelayAcceptsBranchCreate` (asserts the new positive behavior).
- Added `TestCanIncrementalRelayRejectsBranchCreateWithNonZeroTarget` for the incoherent-plan guard.
- Added `TestCanIncrementalRelayMixedCreateAndUpdate` to confirm a mixed plan list still relays.
- Replaced `TestCanIncrementalRelayRejectsNoThin` with `TestCanIncrementalRelayToleratesNoThin`.

`internal/syncer/integration_test.go`:
- Added `TestRun_IntegrationIncrementalRelayCreatesNewBranchWithExistingTarget` — exact reproduction of the user's scenario at unit-test scale: target has master, source adds a new branch, sync should now take incremental relay (not materialized).
- Added `TestRun_IntegrationIncrementalRelayCreatesNewBranchOnNoThinTarget` — same scenario against a no-thin target.
- Updated `TestRun_IntegrationMaterializedLimitFailsClearly` to use `Force: true` to force the materialized path (it previously relied on no-thin to do that).

## End-to-end picture

For your `--tags` sync against the artifacts target, the planner will now:
- Detect `main` already current → skipped, not in pushPlans.
- Detect all the new branches → `ActionCreate` plans, eligible for incremental relay.
- Detect new tags → `ActionCreate` plans, eligible for incremental relay (already were).
- All plans eligible → incremental relay path → source streams a pack with target refs as haves → target receive-pack accepts.

Avoids the materialized-walker rabbit hole entirely. Worth re-running:

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --tags \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

Expect either success or, if there's a separate server-side issue, an error from a much cleaner code path that we can diagnose more directly.

exit status 1

> AGENT

This is the diagnostic-headers patch from earlier doing exactly its job — `Cf-Ray=9f613eac387d3917-TXL`, `Server=cloudflare`, plus the body `Invalid or expired token`. That's a real, actionable error this time.

Two things explain why no credential prompt:

1. **The server returned 403, not 401.** A 401 ("not authenticated") is what triggers credential prompts; a 403 ("token rejected") is the server saying "you sent credentials, they're just no good." So no prompt fires.
2. **Your earlier successful runs cached working credentials in the macOS keychain.** Subsequent invocations are pulling that cached token automatically. It's now expired/invalidated, so the server rejects with 403, and we never get to the prompt path.

## Fix

Clear the cached cloudflare-artifacts credentials and rerun. The hostname to forget is `8b04592ed74a5cce30d355b07276caf3.artifacts.cloudflare.net`.

```sh
printf 'protocol=https\nhost=8b04592ed74a5cce30d355b07276caf3.artifacts.cloudflare.net\n\n' \
  | git credential-osxkeychain erase
```

Or via the GUI: **Keychain Access → search "artifacts.cloudflare.net" → delete the entry**. Next run will prompt fresh.

If your artifacts password is itself a generated token from somewhere (some kind of API token / personal access token from your `entire` account), the underlying issue is that *that* token expired upstream, and you may need to mint a new one before the credential prompt will lead anywhere useful. The 403 body says "Invalid or expired token" — pretty unambiguous.

Once you re-auth, the more interesting question gets answered: does the new incremental-relay-with-creates path actually push your full sync to that target. That's what the previous turn's fix was about, and we'll only know after this auth issue clears.

Good news embedded in the bad news: **the relay fix is working as intended**. The error chain now says `incremental execute` (was `materialized execute` before) — git-sync is routing the multi-create case through streaming incremental relay instead of falling back to the materialized walker. We've shipped the path the user's case needed.

What remains is a plain server-side 500 from Cloudflare's git artifacts receive-pack. We've now ruled out:
- git-sync's materialized walker (fixed and bypassed).
- Pack-content issues (relay forwards the source's own pack — same kind that worked in your single-branch test B).
- Auth (you just authed cleanly).
- Transport (HTTP/1.1; we'd see different errors otherwise).

The diagnostic-headers patch from earlier is doing real work here — `Cf-Ray=9f614186d8dfd81e-TXL`. **That's the actionable artifact.** Hand it to whoever runs the artifacts service (or open a ticket with Cloudflare if they own it). They can look up that ray ID and see exactly what their receive-pack did before bailing with a generic 500. The body just says "Internal Server Error" because their handler didn't bother shaping a response — but the Cf-Ray gives them everything they need on the server side.

A couple last things worth checking on your end before sending it off:

1. **Try `--verbose`** in case any sideband progress slips through before the 500 — sometimes the server logs a useful message via sideband even when its HTTP response is generic:
   ```sh

     https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
   ```
2. **Try a vanilla `git push --mirror`** to confirm the same target rejects the same content from a different client. If vanilla git also 500s, fully a server-side problem. If vanilla git succeeds, there's still some subtle git-sync difference to hunt down — but I'd be surprised given we're now using a source-relayed pack.

For your original question — "validate sync is working" — I'd consider that answered. You've successfully demonstrated:
- Single-branch sync via bootstrap (test B). ✓
- No-op sync via incremental (test A). ✓
- Materialized walker handles transitive-missing objects correctly (the first fix). ✓
- Incremental relay handles branch creates with non-empty target (the second fix). ✓

The only remaining failure is on the artifacts server, and it's emitting a Cf-Ray for someone else to investigate. Submit the ID; this isn't ours to fix.

> AGENT

Useful new data. Two observations:

**1. The pack is large.** `Total 17837 (delta 3004), reused 1841 (delta 1819), pack-reused 14290` — ~17.8K objects, partly delta-compressed, in a single push. That's a sizable receive-pack body. The artifacts service may have a body-size cap or a backend timeout that fires on packs of this size, and Cloudflare returns a generic 500 when its origin times out or rejects.

**2. There are two `source: Enumerating objects: 17837` sequences.** That's a separate cosmetic/efficiency issue — git-sync is fetching from the source twice:
- First, `FetchToStore` at `internal/syncer/syncer.go:497` populates the in-memory store for plan building.
- Then `FetchPack` inside the incremental relay path fetches the same closure again to stream to the target.

The double fetch doesn't cause your 500, but it's wasteful and worth fixing in a follow-up. (For incremental relay specifically, we could skip the local-store fetch entirely if all plans are relay-eligible.)

## Try `--target-max-pack-bytes`

The artifacts server's pack-size limit is the most likely 500 trigger. git-sync supports auto-batching when the target rejects a too-large body, but only for the bootstrap path. For incremental relay, you'd set the cap explicitly:

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync -v --tags \
  --target-max-pack-bytes 10485760 \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

(10 MiB. Drop to 1 MiB if it still fails.)

If that succeeds, we know it was a size limit. If it fails with the same shape of error at much smaller sizes, the artifacts server has a different problem.

## What to share with the artifacts service operators

Two Cf-Ray IDs from this thread are particularly diagnostic:
- `9f614186d8dfd81e-TXL` — earlier 500 on a similar-sized push.
- `9f6145de4e3b236c-TXL` — this one.

Plus the body content (`Internal Server Error`) is generic — they should be able to look up *their* logs by Cf-Ray and see what happened on receive-pack. The pattern (large push → generic 500) is the hint they need.

## Do you want me to fix the double-fetch?

For incremental-relay-only paths, the in-memory store fetch is redundant. The plan can be built from refs alone for branch creates (no ancestry walk needed) and the existing FF-update path needs a small subset. Worth a follow-up — but it's a real change to the syncer flow, not a one-line tweak. Say the word and I'll plan it out.

> AGENT

I was wrong about `--target-max-pack-bytes` helping the incremental path — it only triggers batching in bootstrap. The flag was a no-op for your run; the pack was still 17.8K objects.

Looking at the output, also note both fetches reported `Total 17837` — the same 17K-object closure that took the streaming relay path is what the artifacts server is choking on. Server keeps returning a generic 500 with no useful body. Three new Cf-Ray IDs to share now (`9f614186…`, `9f6145de…`, `9f616a23…`).

## What actually works to get this to succeed

**Option A — Manual per-branch loop (works today, no code change).** Single-branch invocations take the bootstrap path (no managed refs in scope per call) and we already verified those work for that target:

```sh
git -C /tmp/cli-probe for-each-ref --format='%(refname:short)' refs/heads/ | grep -v '^main$' | while read b; do
  echo "=== $b ==="
  GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --branch "$b" \
    https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
done
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync --tags \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

(Cache the credential helper first to avoid prompting per call.) Slow but functional. Confirms the issue is volume.

**Option B — Proper code fix.** Make the user's exact scenario eligible for **batched bootstrap relay**, which:
- Splits the closure into checkpoints sized to `--target-max-pack-bytes`.
- Uses target refs as haves on each batch (already implemented at `bootstrap.go:225`).
- Already auto-retries with auto-batching when a target rejects a too-large body (`bootstrap.go:138`).

The block is `CanBootstrapRelay` rejecting whenever *any* desired ref exists on target — even when that ref is already at the source's hash (a no-op). For your case, target's `main` is at the same hash as source's `main`, so the desired list contains a "match." Relaxing the check to allow already-current matches (treating them as already-bootstrapped) routes you straight into the batched path.

Two pieces have to move together:
- `CanBootstrapRelay` (`internal/planner/relay.go:34`) — only reject when target's hash for a desired ref is **non-zero and differs from the desired source hash**.
- `BuildBootstrapPlans` (`internal/planner/planner.go:233`) — same: skip already-current refs as `ActionSkip`, only emit `ActionCreate` for the rest.

Plus a couple of integration tests covering the "target has main at correct hash, source adds branches" case end-to-end, and a planner test for the relaxed `CanBootstrapRelay`.

This is the right shape of fix and it's the natural extension of what we did today. It's a self-contained change but a bigger one than the previous two — touches the bootstrap relay decision logic, plan building, and there are several integration tests around bootstrap eligibility that I'd want to read carefully before changing the rules.

Want me to implement Option B?

but we are still mostly guessing that this is the issue or the fix is worth is no matter what?

> AGENT

You're right to push back. Let me be honest about what we actually know vs. what I'm guessing.

## What we actually know

- Target returns 500 with body `Internal Server Error`. No further detail from the server.
- Single-branch sync via bootstrap relay works (test B, one data point).
- No-op single-branch sync works (test A, irrelevant to push behavior).
- Full sync with 17837 objects in the pack fails.
- Three Cf-Ray IDs the server-side operators could look up.

## What I'm guessing

- That the failure is size-related. The verbose output gives object count, not byte size. We don't know if the pack is 5 MB or 500 MB.
- That batching would fix it. If Cloudflare's failure is a deterministic backend bug unrelated to size — bad object format, an edge-case in their handler, an auth quirk — then no amount of batching helps.
- That the per-branch loop "works." I extrapolated from one branch.
- That the artifacts service has any size cap at all. Could just be a server crash that happens to correlate with this repo's content.

## Cheap experiments to validate before any implementation

These three would actually tell us where the bug lives:

**1. Vanilla `git push --mirror` to the same target.** If that also 500s, it's a server bug, full stop, and Option B fixes nothing. If it succeeds, git-sync is sending something different.

```sh
cd /tmp/cli-probe
git push --mirror $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

**2. Two branches in one git-sync invocation.** Tests whether the threshold is "exactly 1 ref" or "many refs":

```sh
GODEBUG=http2client=0 go run cmd/git-sync/main.go sync \
  --branch 1072-intermittent-stop-hook-timeout-after-30s-on-windows-with-concurrent-codexclaude-sessions \
  --branch agent-improvements \
  https://github.com/entireio/cli.git $ARTIFACTS_REMOTE
```

**3. Pack byte size.** Run vanilla `git pack-objects` locally with the same wants/haves to see how big the pack actually is. If it's <10 MB, "size limit" is implausible.

The most informative thing of all: **share a Cf-Ray with whoever runs the artifacts service.** They'll know in 30 seconds. We've been reverse-engineering through a 500 with no body — that's a losing game when we can just ask.

## My honest recommendation

Don't implement Option B yet. The fixes already shipped (materialized walker, incremental relay for creates) are independently correct improvements. The server-side 500 is a separate problem and we haven't proven it's a git-sync problem at all. Run experiments 1 and 2, share a Cf-Ray with the server team — *then* decide if more code change is justified.

```
@@ -16,6 +16,21 @@ import (
 
 const maxHTTPErrorBody = 64 * 1024
 
+// diagnosticHeaders carry trace/correlation IDs that operators of upstream
+// services use to look up the failing request server-side. Surfaced in
+// httpError so a 500 with an opaque body (e.g. "Internal Server Error") still
+// gives the user something actionable to share when reporting the failure.
+var diagnosticHeaders = []string{
+	"Cf-Ray",
+	"X-Request-Id",
+	"Request-Id",
+	"X-Trace-Id",
+	"X-Amz-Request-Id",
+	"X-Github-Request-Id",
+	"Server",
+	"Content-Type",
+}
+
 // httpError checks an HTTP response status and returns an error for non-2xx responses.
 func httpError(res *http.Response) error {
 	if res.StatusCode >= http.StatusOK && res.StatusCode < http.StatusMultipleChoices {
@@ -29,9 +44,18 @@ func httpError(res *http.Response) error {
 			if len(data) > maxHTTPErrorBody {
 				data = append(data[:maxHTTPErrorBody], []byte("...")...)
 			}
-			reason = string(data)
+			reason = strings.TrimSpace(string(data))
 		}
 	}
+	var diag []string
+	for _, h := range diagnosticHeaders {
+		if v := res.Header.Get(h); v != "" {
+			diag = append(diag, h+"="+v)
+		}
+	}
+	if len(diag) > 0 {
+		return fmt.Errorf("http %d: %s [%s] %s", res.StatusCode, res.Request.URL.Redacted(), strings.Join(diag, ", "), reason)
+	}
 	return fmt.Errorf("http %d: %s %s", res.StatusCode, res.Request.URL.Redacted(), reason)
 }
```

> AGENT

Committed as `9c95280` on `soph/fixes`. Tree is clean — ready for you to push and open the PR.

When you're back for the double-fetch follow-up: the redundant call is `FetchToStore` at `internal/syncer/syncer.go:497`, which populates the in-memory store for plan building before `incremental.Execute` does its own `FetchPack` to stream to the target. For pure relay paths (all plans relay-eligible) the local store fetch is unnecessary — plan building for branch creates and FF updates only needs ref-level data and ancestry checks, not the full closure. Skipping it would halve the source-side bandwidth on relay-only syncs.

> DEVELOPER

can you give me a super short pr description

> AGENT

Sure:

> Three fixes for syncing into targets that share reachability with the source.
>
> - **Materialized walker tolerates pruned objects.** A fetch with target refs as haves prunes the closure server-side; the walker now treats missing references (top-level *and* transitive) as implicitly have'd by the target instead of failing with `object not found`.
> - **Incremental relay accepts branch creates.** Previously fell through to materialized when target had any existing ref and source added a new branch. Also tolerates `no-thin` targets — same rationale as replicate relay (the source pack is always self-contained).
> - **`httpError` surfaces diagnostic headers** (`Cf-Ray`, `Server`, `X-Request-Id`, …) so an opaque 500 leaves something to share with the upstream operator.

```
461		measurementDone := s.measurementDone

473		}
474	
475		// Check for bootstrap opportunity (before allocating in-memory repo)
476		if ok, reason := planner.CanBootstrapRelay(s.cfg.Force, s.cfg.Prune, desiredRefs, targetRefMap); ok {

484					OperationMode: modeSync, BootstrapSuggested: true, Stats: stats.snapshot(),
485					Measurement: measurementDone(), Protocol: sourceService.Protocol,
486				}, nil
487			}
488			return bootstrapWithInputs(ctx, s, desiredRefs, targetRefMap, reason)
489		}
490	
491		// Normal sync: allocate in-memory repo and fetch objects
492		repo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage(), nil)

495		}

497		if err := sourceService.FetchToStore(ctx, repo.Storer, s.sourceConn, gpDesired, targetRefMap); err != nil {
498			if !errors.Is(err, git.NoErrAlreadyUpToDate) {
499				return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("fetch to store: %w", err)
500			}
501		}
502	
503		plans, err := planner.BuildPlans(repo.Storer, desiredRefs, targetRefMap, managedTargets, planConfig(s.cfg))
504		if err != nil {
505			return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("build plans: %w", err)
506		}
507	
508		result := Result{
509			Plans: plans, DryRun: s.cfg.DryRun, OperationMode: modeSync, Protocol: sourceService.Protocol,
510			Stats: stats.snapshot(), Measurement: measurementDone(),

512

539			}

541				result.Relay = incResult.Relay

550		}
551	
552		for _, plan := range pushPlans {
553			switch plan.Action {

556			case ActionDelete:

558			case ActionSkip, ActionBlock:
559				// not applicable in this context
```

```
98	// FetchCommitGraph fetches only the commit graph (tree:0 filter) for a ref.
99	// Requires v2 with filter support. Optional haves let the source skip commits
100	// already reachable from those hashes, which is valuable when planning later
101	// branches that share history with an already-planned trunk.
102	func (s *RefService) FetchCommitGraph(
103		ctx context.Context,
104		store storer.Storer,
105		conn *Conn,
106		ref DesiredRef,
107		haves []plumbing.Hash,
108	) error {
109		if s.Protocol != "v2" {
110			return errors.New("commit graph fetch requires protocol v2")
111		}
112		if !s.V2Caps.FetchSupports("filter") {
113			return errors.New("source does not advertise fetch filter support")
114		}
115	
116		sortedHaves := SortedUniqueHashes(haves)
117		cmdArgs := make([]string, 0, 4+len(sortedHaves))
118		cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs,
119			"ofs-delta",
120			"no-progress",
121			"filter tree:0",
122			"want "+ref.SourceHash.String(),
123		)
124		for _, h := range sortedHaves {
125			cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "have "+h.String())
126		}
127		cmdArgs = append(cmdArgs, "done")
128	
129		body, err := EncodeCommand("fetch", s.V2Caps.RequestCapabilities(), cmdArgs)
130		if err != nil {
131			return err
132		}
133		reader, err := PostRPCStream(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, body, true, "upload-pack fetch")
134		if err != nil {
135			return err
136		}
137		defer ioutil.CheckClose(reader, &err)
138		// Commit-graph fetches are short and not user-facing; skip progress.
139		return storeV2FetchPack(store, reader, false)
140	}
141	
142	// Capabilities returns the sorted capability list for display.
143	func (s *RefService) Capabilities() []string {
144		switch s.Protocol {
145		case "v2":
146			return s.V2Caps.SortedKeys()
147		case "v1":
148			return AdvRefsCaps(s.V1Adv)
149		default:
150			return nil
151		}
152	}
```

```
263	
264	// PlanRef determines the action for a single ref that exists on both source and target.
265	func PlanRef(store storer.EncodedObjectStorer, want DesiredRef, targetHash plumbing.Hash, force bool) (BranchPlan, error) {
266		plan := BranchPlan{
267			Branch:     want.Label,
268			SourceRef:  want.SourceRef,
269			TargetRef:  want.TargetRef,
270			SourceHash: want.SourceHash,
271			TargetHash: targetHash,
272			Kind:       want.Kind,
273		}
274	
275		if want.SourceHash == targetHash {
276			plan.Action = ActionSkip
277			plan.Reason = ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " already current"
278			return plan, nil
279		}
280	
281		if want.Kind == RefKindTag {
282			if force {
283				plan.Action = ActionUpdate
284				plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " (force tag update)"
285				return plan, nil
286			}
287			plan.Action = ActionBlock
288			plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " differs from " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + "; use --force to retarget tag"
289			return plan, nil
290		}
291	
292		isFF, err := ReachesCommit(store, want.SourceHash, targetHash)
293		if err != nil {
294			if errors.Is(err, ErrAncestryDepthExceeded) {
295				// Can't prove fast-forward within depth limit — block with explanation.
296				plan.Action = ActionBlock
297				plan.Reason = "ancestry check for " + want.TargetRef.String() + " exceeded depth limit; use --force if this is a valid fast-forward"
298				return plan, nil
299			}
300			return plan, fmt.Errorf("check fast-forward for %s: %w", want.TargetRef, err)
301		}
302		if isFF {
303			plan.Action = ActionUpdate
304			plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash)
305			return plan, nil
306		}
307	
308		if force {
309			plan.Action = ActionUpdate
310			plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " (force)"
311			return plan, nil
312		}
313	
314		plan.Action = ActionBlock
315		plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " is not an ancestor of " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash)
316		return plan, nil
317	}
318	
319	// PlanReplicationRef determines the overwrite-oriented action for a single ref.
320	func PlanReplicationRef(want DesiredRef, targetHash plumbing.Hash, existsOnTarget bool) BranchPlan {
321		plan := BranchPlan{
322			Branch:     want.Label,
323			SourceRef:  want.SourceRef,
324			TargetRef:  want.TargetRef,
325			SourceHash: want.SourceHash,
326			TargetHash: targetHash,
327			Kind:       want.Kind,
328		}
329	
330		if want.SourceHash == targetHash {
331			plan.Action = ActionSkip
332			plan.Reason = ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " already current"
333			return plan
334		}
335	
336		if !existsOnTarget || targetHash.IsZero() {
337			plan.Action = ActionCreate
338			plan.Reason = ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " -> <new>"
339			return plan
340		}
341	
342		plan.Action = ActionUpdate
343		switch want.Kind {
344		case RefKindTag:
345			plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " (replicate tag overwrite)"
346		case RefKindBranch:
347			plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " (replicate overwrite)"
348		default:
349			plan.Reason = ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> " + ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " (replicate overwrite)"
350		}
351		return plan
352	}
353	
354	// MaxAncestryDepth is the maximum number of commits to visit during a
355	// fast-forward ancestry check. This prevents full graph walks on very
356	// large histories (issue #16). Set high enough to avoid false negatives
357	// on real repos — even the Linux kernel has ~1.3M commits.
358	const MaxAncestryDepth = 2_000_000
359	
360	// ErrAncestryDepthExceeded is returned when ReachesCommit exceeds MaxAncestryDepth.
361	var ErrAncestryDepthExceeded = errors.New("ancestry check exceeded depth limit")
362
```

```
95		managed map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget,

97	) ([]BranchPlan, error) {
98		if cfg.Prune {
99			for targetRef := range targetRefs {
100				if _, ok := managed[targetRef]; ok {
101					continue
102				}
103				switch {
104				case targetRef.IsTag() && cfg.IncludeTags:
105					managed[targetRef] = ManagedTarget{Kind: RefKindTag, Label: targetRef.Short()}
106				case targetRef.IsBranch() && len(cfg.Mappings) == 0 && len(cfg.Branches) == 0:
107					managed[targetRef] = ManagedTarget{Kind: RefKindBranch, Label: targetRef.Short()}
108				}
109			}
110		}
111	
112		targetNames := make([]plumbing.ReferenceName, 0, len(managed))
113		for name := range managed {
114			targetNames = append(targetNames, name)
115		}
116		sort.Slice(targetNames, func(i, j int) bool { return targetNames[i] < targetNames[j] })
117	
118		plans := make([]BranchPlan, 0, len(targetNames))
119		for _, targetRef := range targetNames {
120			info := managed[targetRef]
121			want, existsInDesired := desired[targetRef]
122			targetHash, existsOnTarget := targetRefs[targetRef]
123	
124			if !existsInDesired {
125				if cfg.Prune && existsOnTarget {
126					plans = append(plans, BranchPlan{
127						Branch:     info.Label,
128						TargetRef:  targetRef,
129						TargetHash: targetHash,
130						Kind:       info.Kind,
131						Action:     ActionDelete,
132						Reason:     ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> <deleted>",
133					})
134				}
135				continue
136			}
137	
138			if !existsOnTarget {
139				plans = append(plans, BranchPlan{
140					Branch:     want.Label,
141					SourceRef:  want.SourceRef,
142					TargetRef:  want.TargetRef,
143					SourceHash: want.SourceHash,
144					Kind:       want.Kind,
145					Action:     ActionCreate,
146					Reason:     ShortHash(want.SourceHash) + " -> <new>",
147				})
148				continue
149			}
150	
151			plan, err := PlanRef(store, want, targetHash, cfg.Force)
152			if err != nil {
153				return nil, err
154			}
155			plans = append(plans, plan)
156		}
157	
158		sort.Slice(plans, func(i, j int) bool {
159			return plans[i].TargetRef.String() < plans[j].TargetRef.String()
160		})
161		return plans, nil
162	}
163	
164	// BuildReplicationPlans generates overwrite-oriented plans for replication mode.
165	// Divergent refs are updated directly rather than blocked.
166	//
167	//nolint:unparam // error return kept for API consistency with BuildPlans
168	func BuildReplicationPlans(
169		desired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]DesiredRef,
170		targetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,
171		managed map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget,
172		cfg PlanConfig,
173	) ([]BranchPlan, error) {
174		managed = copyManagedTargets(managed)
175		if cfg.Prune {
176			for targetRef := range targetRefs {
177				if _, ok := managed[targetRef]; ok {
178					continue
179				}
180				switch {
181				case targetRef.IsTag() && cfg.IncludeTags:
182					managed[targetRef] = ManagedTarget{Kind: RefKindTag, Label: targetRef.Short()}
183				case targetRef.IsBranch() && len(cfg.Mappings) == 0 && len(cfg.Branches) == 0:
184					managed[targetRef] = ManagedTarget{Kind: RefKindBranch, Label: targetRef.Short()}
185				}
186			}
187		}
188	
189		targetNames := make([]plumbing.ReferenceName, 0, len(managed))
190		for name := range managed {
191			targetNames = append(targetNames, name)
192		}
193		sort.Slice(targetNames, func(i, j int) bool { return targetNames[i] < targetNames[j] })
194	
195		plans := make([]BranchPlan, 0, len(targetNames))
196		for _, targetRef := range targetNames {
197			info := managed[targetRef]
198			want, existsInDesired := desired[targetRef]
199			targetHash, existsOnTarget := targetRefs[targetRef]
200	
201			if !existsInDesired {
202				if cfg.Prune && existsOnTarget {
203					plans = append(plans, BranchPlan{
204						Branch:     info.Label,
205						TargetRef:  targetRef,
206						TargetHash: targetHash,
207						Kind:       info.Kind,
208						Action:     ActionDelete,
209						Reason:     ShortHash(targetHash) + " -> <deleted>",
210					})
211				}
212				continue
213			}
214	
215			plans = append(plans, PlanReplicationRef(want, targetHash, existsOnTarget))
216		}
217	
218		sort.Slice(plans, func(i, j int) bool {
219			return plans[i].TargetRef.String() < plans[j].TargetRef.String()
220		})
221		return plans, nil
222	}
223	
224	func copyManagedTargets(input map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget) map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget {
225		out := make(map[plumbing.ReferenceName]ManagedTarget, len(input))
226		for k, v := range input {
227			out[k] = v
228		}
229		return out
230	}
231

236	) ([]BranchPlan, error) {

247				return nil, fmt.Errorf("target ref %s already exists; use sync for non-bootstrap runs", targetRef)
```

> AGENT

Quick clarification before I dig in: the `soph/prevent-double-fetch` branch is currently at `main` (`7ac0134`), while the fixes you just committed for the previous PR are on `soph/fixes` (`9c95280`).

The double-fetch optimization is most meaningful **layered on top of `soph/fixes`** — without those changes, branch creates with an existing target still fall through to materialized (which legitimately needs the full store), so skipping `FetchToStore` would break that path. The optimization really only pays off once relay handles the create case.

Two ways to set this up:

1. **Rebase `soph/prevent-double-fetch` on `soph/fixes`.** Cleanest. The PR for this branch will sit on top of the previous PR. If you merge them in order, the dependency stays clean.
2. **Independent of `soph/fixes`.** Doable, but then I need to scope the fix to only kick in for plans that don't need full closure (FF updates without store-required ancestry checks via `FetchCommitGraph`), and we need a fallback for servers without v2+filter. More code, narrower benefit until both PRs merge.

Which do you want? My recommendation is (1) — rebase this branch on `soph/fixes`, fix is straightforward and meaningful, and the PR descriptions can note the dependency.

```json
  "new_string": "func planConfig(cfg Config) planner.PlanConfig {\n\treturn planner.PlanConfig{\n\t\tBranches:    cfg.Branches,\n\t\tMappings:    cfg.Mappings,\n\t\tIncludeTags: cfg.IncludeTags,\n\t\tForce:       cfg.Force,\n\t\tPrune:       cfg.Prune,\n\t}\n}\n\n// needsLocalSourceClosure reports whether the sync must populate the\n// in-memory store with the full source closure before running. The fetch\n// is required when:\n//   - Force or prune is set: incremental relay is disabled, so the\n//     materialized fallback will run and needs the closure.\n//   - Any desired ref already exists on target at a different hash: a\n//     branch fast-forward check will need ancestry data, or a tag retarget\n//     will route to materialized.\n//\n// When all desired refs are either skips (target hash matches source) or\n// creates (target hash is zero), incremental relay handles the push without\n// the closure — the upfront fetch would just be wasted bandwidth, since\n// relay does its own FetchPack on the source.\nfunc needsLocalSourceClosure(\n\tcfg Config,\n\tdesired map[plumbing.ReferenceName]planner.DesiredRef,\n\ttargetRefs map[plumbing.ReferenceName]plumbing.Hash,\n) bool {\n\tif cfg.Force || cfg.Prune {\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\tfor targetRef, want := range desired {\n\t\ttargetHash := targetRefs[targetRef]\n\t\tif targetHash.IsZero() {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif targetHash == want.SourceHash {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}"
```

```
176	// TestRun_IntegrationIncrementalRelayCreatesNewBranchWithExistingTarget verifies
177	// that when target already has a managed ref (so bootstrap is ineligible) and
178	// source adds a new branch, the sync takes the incremental relay path rather
179	// than falling through to materialized. The relay uses target refs as haves,
180	// so the source pack is minimal and self-contained.
181	func TestRun_IntegrationIncrementalRelayCreatesNewBranchWithExistingTarget(t *testing.T) {
182		sourceRepo, sourceFS := newSourceRepo(t)
183		makeCommits(t, sourceRepo, sourceFS, 3)
184	
185		targetRepo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage())
186		if err != nil {
187			t.Fatalf("init target repo: %v", err)
188		}
189		if err := copyRefsAndObjects(sourceRepo.Storer, targetRepo.Storer, []plumbing.ReferenceName{plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(testBranch)}); err != nil {
190			t.Fatalf("copy target baseline: %v", err)
191		}
192	
193		// Add a new branch on source pointing at the same commit target already
194		// has via master. With the old planner this forced materialized and
195		// produced a thin push that some receive-pack servers reject; now it
196		// should relay with all target refs as haves.
197		sourceHead, err := sourceRepo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(testBranch), true)
198		if err != nil {
199			t.Fatalf("resolve source head: %v", err)
200		}
201		releaseRef := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName("release")
202		if err := sourceRepo.Storer.SetReference(plumbing.NewHashReference(releaseRef, sourceHead.Hash())); err != nil {
203			t.Fatalf("set source release branch: %v", err)
204		}
205	
206		sourceServer := newSmartHTTPRepoServerV2(t, sourceRepo)
207		targetServer := newSmartHTTPRepoServer(t, targetRepo)
208		targetServer.receivePackThinCap = true
209		defer sourceServer.Close()
210		defer targetServer.Close()
211	
212		result, err := Run(context.Background(), Config{
213			Source:       Endpoint{URL: sourceServer.RepoURL()},
214			Target:       Endpoint{URL: targetServer.RepoURL()},
215			ProtocolMode: protocolModeAuto,
216		})
217		if err != nil {
218			t.Fatalf("sync: %v", err)
219		}
220		if !result.Relay || result.RelayMode != relayModeIncremental {
221			t.Fatalf("expected incremental relay, got mode=%q reason=%q relay=%v", result.RelayMode, result.RelayReason, result.Relay)
222		}
223		if result.Pushed != 1 {
224			t.Fatalf("expected 1 ref pushed (release create), got %d", result.Pushed)
225		}
226	
227		gotRelease, err := targetRepo.Reference(releaseRef, true)
228		if err != nil {
229			t.Fatalf("resolve target release ref: %v", err)
230		}
231		if gotRelease.Hash() != sourceHead.Hash() {
232			t.Fatalf("target release hash = %s, want %s", gotRelease.Hash(), sourceHead.Hash())
233		}
234	}

237	// no-thin variant of the above: the relayed pack is always self-contained
238	// (gitproto.FetchPack never sets thin-pack), so a no-thin receive-pack
239	// accepts it just fine.
240	func TestRun_IntegrationIncrementalRelayCreatesNewBranchOnNoThinTarget(t *testing.T) {
```

```json
  "new_string": "// TestRun_IntegrationSkipsLocalFetchOnRelayOnlySync verifies the\n// double-fetch optimization: when every desired ref is a skip or a create\n// (no FF check needed, no force, no prune), the upfront FetchToStore that\n// populates the in-memory store is skipped entirely. The incremental relay\n// still does its own FetchPack to stream to target, so we end up with one\n// source upload-pack call instead of two.\nfunc TestRun_IntegrationSkipsLocalFetchOnRelayOnlySync(t *testing.T) {\n\tsourceRepo, sourceFS := newSourceRepo(t)\n\tmakeCommits(t, sourceRepo, sourceFS, 3)\n\n\ttargetRepo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage())\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"init target repo: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := copyRefsAndObjects(sourceRepo.Storer, targetRepo.Storer, []plumbing.ReferenceName{plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(testBranch)}); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"copy target baseline: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Source adds a new branch reachable from master (which target already\n\t// has). All plans are skip (master) or create (release) — no FF check\n\t// is needed and incremental relay handles the push directly.\n\tsourceHead, err := sourceRepo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(testBranch), true)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"resolve source head: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\treleaseRef := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(\"release\")\n\tif err := sourceRepo.Storer.SetReference(plumbing.NewHashReference(releaseRef, sourceHead.Hash())); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"set source release branch: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tsourceServer := newSmartHTTPRepoServerV2(t, sourceRepo)\n\ttargetServer := newSmartHTTPRepoServer(t, targetRepo)\n\ttargetServer.receivePackThinCap = true\n\tdefer sourceServer.Close()\n\tdefer targetServer.Close()\n\n\tresult, err := Run(context.Background(), Config{\n\t\tSource:       Endpoint{URL: sourceServer.RepoURL()},\n\t\tTarget:       Endpoint{URL: targetServer.RepoURL()},\n\t\tProtocolMode: protocolModeAuto,\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"sync: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !result.Relay || result.RelayMode != relayModeIncremental {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected incremental relay, got mode=%q reason=%q relay=%v\", result.RelayMode, result.RelayReason, result.Relay)\n\t}\n\n\t// One upload-pack call: the relay's FetchPack. The pre-fix flow would\n\t// have done two (FetchToStore + FetchPack).\n\tif got := sourceServer.Count(serviceUploadPack, metricPack); got != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected exactly 1 source upload-pack call, got %d\", got)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestRun_IntegrationKeepsLocalFetchWhenAncestryNeeded ensures the fetch is\n// still performed for a fast-forward update where BuildPlans calls\n// ReachesCommit on the local store. Skipping it would crash the planner.\nfunc TestRun_IntegrationKeepsLocalFetchWhenAncestryNeeded(t *testing.T) {\n\tsourceRepo, sourceFS := newSourceRepo(t)\n\tmakeCommits(t, sourceRepo, sourceFS, 2)\n\n\ttargetRepo, _ := newSourceRepo(t)\n\n\tsourceServer := newSmartHTTPRepoServer(t, sourceRepo)\n\ttargetServer := newSmartHTTPRepoServer(t, targetRepo)\n\ttargetServer.receivePackThinCap = true\n\tdefer sourceServer.Close()\n\tdefer targetServer.Close()\n\n\tcfg := Config{\n\t\tSource: Endpoint{URL: sourceServer.RepoURL()},\n\t\tTarget: Endpoint{URL: targetServer.RepoURL()},\n\t}\n\n\tif _, err := Run(context.Background(), cfg); err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"seed sync: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Advance source so the resync plans an FF update.\n\tmakeCommits(t, sourceRepo, sourceFS, 1)\n\n\tresult, err := Run(context.Background(), cfg)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"resync: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !result.Relay || result.RelayMode != relayModeIncremental {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected incremental relay, got mode=%q reason=%q\", result.RelayMode, result.RelayReason)\n\t}\n\tif result.Pushed != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected 1 ref pushed, got %+v\", result)\n\t}\n}\n\n// TestRun_IntegrationIncrementalRelayCreatesNewBranchOnNoThinTarget covers the"
}
```

```
19	// RefService encapsulates the result of source ref discovery and the negotiated
20	// protocol, providing methods for subsequent fetch and pack operations.
21	type RefService struct {
22		Protocol string // "v1" or "v2"
23		V1Adv    *packp.AdvRefs
24		V2Caps   *V2Capabilities
25		// HeadTarget is the branch that HEAD points to on the source, when
26		// advertised as a symref. Empty for detached HEAD or when the source
27		// does not advertise symref information.
28		HeadTarget plumbing.ReferenceName
29		// Verbose, when true, streams source-side sideband progress ("Counting
30		// objects", "Compressing objects", ...) to stderr and asks the source
31		// upload-pack to emit progress by not sending the no-progress option.
32		Verbose bool
33	}
34	
35	// ListSourceRefs discovers refs from the source using the configured protocol mode.
36	// Returns the list of refs and a RefService for subsequent operations.
37	func ListSourceRefs(ctx context.Context, conn *Conn, protocolMode string, refPrefixes []string) ([]*plumbing.Reference, *RefService, error) {
38		switch protocolMode {
39		case "v1":
40			adv, refs, err := listSourceRefsV1(ctx, conn)
41			if err != nil {
42				return nil, nil, err
43			}
44			return refs, &RefService{Protocol: "v1", V1Adv: adv, HeadTarget: headTargetFromAdv(adv)}, nil
45	
46		case "auto", "v2":
47			data, err := RequestInfoRefs(ctx, conn, transport.UploadPackService, "version=2")
48			if err != nil {
49				return nil, nil, err
50			}
51			if caps, err := DecodeV2Capabilities(bytes.NewReader(data)); err == nil {
52				if !caps.Supports("ls-refs") || !caps.Supports("fetch") {
53					return nil, nil, errors.New("source does not advertise required protocol v2 commands")
54				}
55				refs, headTarget, err := listSourceRefsV2(ctx, conn, caps, refPrefixes)
56				if err != nil {
57					return nil, nil, err
58				}
59				return refs, &RefService{Protocol: "v2", V2Caps: caps, HeadTarget: headTarget}, nil
60			}
61			if protocolMode == "v2" {
62				return nil, nil, errors.New("source did not negotiate protocol v2")
63			}
64			// Fall back to v1
65			adv, err := decodeV1AdvRefs(data)
66			if err != nil {
67				return nil, nil, err
68			}
69			refs, err := AdvRefsToSlice(adv)
70			if err != nil {
71				return nil, nil, err
72			}
73			return refs, &RefService{Protocol: "v1", V1Adv: adv, HeadTarget: headTargetFromAdv(adv)}, nil
74	
75		default:
76			return nil, nil, fmt.Errorf("unsupported protocol mode %q", protocolMode)
77		}
78	}
79	
80	// AdvertisedRefsV1 fetches and decodes v1 advertised refs for the given service.
```

```json
{
  "command": "git add internal/syncer/syncer.go internal/syncer/integration_test.go && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nSkip upfront source fetch when relay handles the push\n\nThe sync flow fetched the full source closure into an in-memory store\nbefore deciding which strategy would run. For relay-only paths the\nstrategy then fetched the same closure again from source to stream\nto the target — every relay-eligible sync paid for the source pack\ntwice.\n\nSkip the upfront FetchToStore when:\n\n- Force and prune are off (otherwise relay is disabled and materialized\n  needs the closure).\n- No desired ref already exists on target at a different hash (otherwise\n  BuildPlans needs ancestry data for fast-forward detection, or the\n  divergent ref will route to materialized).\n\nIn that case all plans are skips, creates, or tag-creates — incremental\nrelay handles them via streaming FetchPack and the local store goes\nunread. The conservative check leaves the prior path in place for any\ncase that might actually need the closure (force, prune, FF updates,\ntag retargets).\n\nVerified end-to-end: TestRun_IntegrationSkipsLocalFetchOnRelayOnlySync\nasserts exactly one upload-pack fetch on a target-has-master /\nsource-adds-release scenario, and TestRun_IntegrationKeepsLocalFetchWhenAncestryNeeded\nensures the FF-update path still has the store populated for\nReachesCommit.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" && git status",
  "description": "Commit the optimization"
}
```

> AGENT

Committed as `ee03e70` on `soph/prevent-double-fetch`. Branch is clean.

## What landed

**`internal/syncer/syncer.go`**
- New helper `needsLocalSourceClosure(cfg, desired, targetRefs)`. Returns true on `Force || Prune` or any divergent ref (target has it at a different hash than source).
- The `FetchToStore` call in `runSync` is now gated by that helper. When false, the in-memory store stays empty — `BuildPlans` doesn't touch it (no FF check needed), and incremental relay does its own `FetchPack` to stream straight to target.

**`internal/syncer/integration_test.go`**
- `TestRun_IntegrationSkipsLocalFetchOnRelayOnlySync`: target has master, source adds a new branch reachable from master. Asserts exactly 1 want across all source upload-pack POSTs. Confirmed to fail (got 2 wants) when the optimization is reverted.
- `TestRun_IntegrationKeepsLocalFetchWhenAncestryNeeded`: target seeded with master, source advances master by one commit (FF update). Asserts the relay still completes — exercises the path where the closure *is* needed for FF detection, so the local fetch must remain.

## What this means for your full-sync scenario

For your `cli.git → artifacts` case, the previous PR's verbose output showed two `Enumerating objects: 17837` sequences from source. With this change, only the relay's own fetch happens — one trip to GitHub per relay-only sync instead of two. Doesn't fix the artifacts-server 500 (still server-side), but it halves the source-side bandwidth and round-trip cost for any relay-only run.

> DEVELOPER

can you give me a short pr description?

> AGENT

> Skip the upfront `FetchToStore` when incremental relay will handle the push (no force/prune, no divergent refs). The relay does its own `FetchPack` to stream the source pack to target, so the upfront fetch was redundant — every relay-only sync was paying for the source pack twice.
>
> Verified by `TestRun_IntegrationSkipsLocalFetchOnRelayOnlySync` (asserts exactly 1 want across source upload-pack POSTs in a target-has-master / source-adds-release scenario) and `TestRun_IntegrationKeepsLocalFetchWhenAncestryNeeded` (FF updates still populate the store for `ReachesCommit`).
>
> Depends on #N (the relay-with-existing-target fixes) — without those, branch creates fall through to materialized which legitimately needs the closure.

## Summary

- **`relay.go`** — added `const reasonIncrementalEligible = "fast-forward-branch-or-tag-create"` (fixes goconst), changed the inner switch's `default:` to explicit `case ActionDelete, ActionSkip, ActionBlock:` (fixes exhaustive). The semantics are unchanged: any non-update/non-create action still falls through with `incremental-branch-action-not-update-or-create`.
- **`planner_test.go`** — five literal occurrences of the reason string replaced with the new constant. The single remaining literal is an error-message format string in `t.Fatalf`, which goconst doesn't flag.

Ready to amend or commit on top of `9c95280`, depending on whether you want this folded into the original commit or kept separate.
